# Case Flow Day 5 — Closing & Settlement

**Phase:** Settlement, closing & the litigation fork · **Focus case:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · **Lead Counsel:** Atty. Elijah Thorne

**Objective.** Negotiate Track A to a $40,000 gross recovery with both UM consents and a Limited Release; reduce the liens and disburse a reconciled $20,359.92 net to John Doe; on Track B (condensed) document the impasse and move the file into litigation without losing a deadline; support the suit (mediation, deposition, arbitration) to its award; and close out a dropped practice matter with its Withdrawal Letters.

**Gate to the next day.** Track A: $40,000 gross recovered, liens resolved at $1,240.00 and $4,580.00, Net Sheet and Trust Ledger reconciled to $0.00, closing memo filed · Track B: impasse documented, file in Litigation status, hard-rule calendar audited, award disbursed on the Litigation Net Sheet · practice drop closed out: Withdrawal Letters sent, status Dropped · Case Flow complete

**The file as this day opens.**

- Track A: the file settles. BI Time-Limited Demand served 07/20/2026; Keystone offers $3,500 on 08/05 and tenders the $10,000 limits on 08/12.
- Liens to resolve: Peach State $1,860.00 paid; Riverton Spine & Rehab $6,290.00 billed under its LOP.
- Track B: the same file, back at 08/05/2026. Keystone holds at $3,500 and lets the Time-Limited Demand lapse on 08/21/2026; suit, mediation (03/18/2027) and UM arbitration (06/22/2027) follow.
- A separate practice matter is pending a drop, to be closed out with its Withdrawal Letters.

### 5.1.1 Countering Adjuster Delay Tactics & Gap Pushback

> **Lesson** · Day 5 — Settlement, closing & the litigation fork · Module 5: Closing & Settlement · scored with Negotiation Mastery Check (`d3m2`)  
> **Topic:** The battle map, the first call, and the stall rebuttals  
> **Continuity:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · Atty. Elijah Thorne · $10,000 BI / $20,000 HOST UM / $10,000 REL UM · 14-Day Gap  
> **Theory extracted from:** `D2#16` Negotiating the “First Call” (For Senior CMs) · `D2#19` Settlement Negotiations: The Pitch & the Negotiation Battle Map · `D2#21` The Valuation Baseline: PIP as a Severity Signal · `D2#24` The Valuation Baseline: Strategic PIP “Exhaustion” · `D2#25` Negotiations Initiated: The “Battle” — First Call to Low-Ball · `D2#27` Common Adjuster “Stall Tactics” and Rebuttals · `D2#29` Settlement Negotiations: The “Never” Rule

#### 1 · Visual Layout Description

A battle map across the full slide width: four orange-numbered stations on a navy rail, left to right — ① NET FLOOR ② VALUE DRIVERS ③ THEIR TOP TWO ④ REBUTTAL READY. Station ① carries a mono pill, $12,677.63 GROSS, with a small red note: KEYSTONE LIMIT $10,000. Under station ③ hang two red-bordered danger cards, 14-DAY GAP and “DEGENERATIVE” MRI; under station ④, two cream cards answer them, each with mono source pills: EMS REPORT · CAUSATION LETTER 06/19/2026, and SUMMIT MRI 03/13/2026. Top-right, a navy chip: DEMAND EXPIRES 08/21/2026. The argument: every attack on this file is answered before the phone rings.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Tell Them

*Why:* Keystone will not argue the crash: Robert Smith was cited and the doorbell camera caught him. It will argue the 14-Day Gap, call the MRI degenerative, and stall toward 08/21/2026. A Case Manager who has mapped both attacks, and the documents that answer them, before the first call turns every stall into a record.

1. The battle map comes before the first call: net floor, three value drivers, the adjuster's top two attacks with the documents that answer them, and the rebuttal. Here both attacks are known: the gap and the “degenerative” MRI.
2. Net floor = liens + costs + fee; below it John Doe receives $0. $7,965.00 + $486.75 = $8,451.75; × 1.5 for the one-third fee = $12,677.63 gross, above Keystone's $10,000 limit. Every layer and lien reduction matters.
3. Value drivers: Smith ran the stop sign on video, with an independent witness (the citation helps the demand; whether a jury hears of it depends on the evidence rule); an objective C5-C6 herniation and injection; residual right-arm paresthesia at MMI.
4. Anchor on total Specials, $16,401.40, not MedPay's $5,000; lead with what MedPay bought: urgent care, Lakeside, the MRI that found the herniation. Chiropractic and PT ran under the LOP.
5. Soft lead: confirm Dana Whitfield has the package, point her to the MRI, restate the demand and its 08/21/2026 deadline as Atty. Thorne approved them, ask for her evaluation. What the firm does if she refuses is his to say. After a low number, pause; ask her data.
6. Anchor and hold: the demand stands as Atty. Thorne approved it. Never apologise for it; you make no moves (“never bid against yourself” is the rule he negotiates by). “That's all I have” is never final on day one.
7. Stalls are built to wear you down. Name the stall, answer with facts, ask for a good-faith offer on the undisputed injury. The demand expires 08/21/2026; only Atty. Thorne can move it.
8. A counteroffer can operate as a rejection of a Time-Limited Demand. Log Keystone's $3,500 as a counteroffer, in her exact words. Whether it ended the demand, and whether restating it keeps 08/21/2026 open, are Atty. Thorne's calls (confirm the governing rule).
9. On the gap and “degenerative”, present the record, never your own view of what they mean: EMS report, 02/09–02/13 call-outs, 02/07 receipt, numbness from 02/18, Dr. Reyes's 06/19/2026 Causation Letter, and Dr. Park: “no significant degenerative change at the adjacent levels.”
10. Log the adjuster's exact words as the call ends; report the offer to Atty. Thorne that day. He conveys it to John Doe, whose decision it is.

> ⚠️ **UPL boundary.** You may present the record, ask the adjuster to justify her number, restate the demand Atty. Thorne approved, and log and relay offers. You may not counter, move the number or the deadline, or tell John Doe what an offer is worth or whether to take it.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Show Them

*The battle map for Keystone, built before the phone rings*

1. **Net floor** — Liens $7,965.00 + costs $486.75 = $8,451.75; × 1.5 for the fee = $12,677.63 gross.
2. **Value drivers** — Cited stop-sign violation on video; C5-C6 herniation and an injection; residuals at MMI.
3. **Their top two** — The 14-Day Gap. The “degenerative” MRI.
4. **Rebuttal ready** — EMS report, call-outs, receipt, Causation Letter; Dr. Park's MRI read.

**Operational mapping**

| Trigger Event | ➔ Case Manager Task | ➔ Tool/Interface | ➔ Verification Checkpoint |
|---|---|---|---|
| Keystone receives the demand (07/22/2026) | Build the battle map and compute the net floor | CMS → Financial Ledger → Net Sheet · CMS → Case Notes | Floor $12,677.63 gross on unreduced liens ($12,677.63 – $4,225.88 fee = $8,451.75); each attack matched to a dated document |
| Adjuster calls with an offer | Soft lead, professional pause, ask for her data; restate the approved demand | Call Simulator → AI Adjuster: Dana Whitfield, Keystone Mutual | No counter; terms and the 08/21/2026 deadline unchanged |
| Call ends | Log the offer and the adjuster's exact words; report to Atty. Thorne | CMS → Communications Log · CMS → Tasks → Escalate | Logged and reported the same day, reasons quoted verbatim |
| Adjuster stalls (“waiting on authority”) | Name it and tie it to the deadline in writing | Email Workspace | Same-day email cites 08/21/2026; no open-ended callback accepted |

**Best practices & pitfalls**

- Make the adjuster justify her number with data; silence after a low offer is a tool.
- Stay firm, factual and professional. An angry call is a gift to the carrier.
- Personalize every answer to John Doe's record; a template rebuttal gets a template offer.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: softening the demand to seem reasonable.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: negotiating without knowing the client's net floor.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: anchoring on the $5,000 MedPay figure instead of total Specials.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: Specials that don't reconcile to the ledger. Know what MedPay paid ($5,000.00) for the Net Sheet; whether payer information goes to Keystone is Atty. Thorne's call.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: accepting an open-ended “I'll get back to you.”

#### 3 · Knowledge Check & CM Summary

Verified in Negotiation Mastery Check (taught on Case Flow Day 5) and that day's auto-graded questions.

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Battle map complete | Floor, three value drivers, both attacks and a written rebuttal present |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Floor arithmetic | $7,965.00 + $486.75 = $8,451.75 × 1.5 = $12,677.63 gross, compared with the $10,000 limit |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Rebuttal sourcing | Each attack answered by a named, dated document; Dr. Park's sentence quoted exactly |
| 🤖 AI | **10%** | Log discipline | Offer, date and exact words logged; same-day report to Atty. Thorne |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **20%** | Strategic grasp | Explains why the gap and the MRI are Keystone's whole case, and how the record answers each |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **20%** | UPL boundary | No counter, no valuation to the client, no change to the demand's terms; every offer relayed through Atty. Thorne |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Open with the question Dana Whitfield will ask, not the one we want: “Why did he wait two weeks?” Make the room answer it from documents only, no adjectives.
- Ask the room: “If this file settled for Keystone's full $10,000 and nothing else, what would John Doe net?” On unreduced liens the answer is $0, and it reframes the whole day.
- Watch for trainees who apologise for the gap. It is already explained: EMS, the call-outs, the receipt, the numbness, the Causation Letter. Their tone should be flat and factual.
- Hold the authority line hard. A senior CM may run the first call, but only inside the authority Atty. Thorne set in writing, and never with a number of their own. Keystone's $3,500 is a counteroffer: whether it ended the Time-Limited Demand, and whether restating it keeps 08/21/2026 alive, is Atty. Thorne's legal call, not the CM's.

### 5.1.2 Securing Maximum Gross Policy-Limit Settlements

> **Lesson** · Day 5 — Settlement, closing & the litigation fork · Module 5: Closing & Settlement · scored with Negotiation Mastery Check (`d3m2`)  
> **Topic:** The UM gap, the reductions that don't apply, and closing at limits  
> **Continuity:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · Atty. Elijah Thorne · $10,000 BI / $20,000 HOST UM / $10,000 REL UM · 14-Day Gap  
> **Theory extracted from:** `D2#20` The Valuation Baseline: PIP Set-Off (The Credit) · `D2#22` The Valuation Baseline: The EMC and the Benefit Ceiling · `D2#23` The Valuation Baseline: Subrogation vs. Non-Subrogation · `D2#26` Settlement Negotiations: The Bracketing Technique · `D2#28` Close the Deal: The “Final-Final” and the Paper Trail · `D3#7` UM Settlement: How the Settlement Is Calculated · `D3#8` UM Settlement: Negotiation & Settlement · `D3#9` UM Settlement: The “Offset” Rule & the Timeline · `D3#10` UM Settlement: Challenges · `D3#11` UM Settlement: The Final Payout Process · `D3#17` Skill Building: UM Settlement — The Bad-Faith Arbitrage & the Permanent Deficit

#### 1 · Visual Layout Description

Three rungs build left to right: BI $10,000 (navy, ticked TENDERED 08/12), HOST UM $20,000 (navy-soft), RELATIVE UM $10,000 (orange), with a running-total pill above each ($10,000 → $30,000 → $40,000) ending in a large Fraunces $40,000 GROSS. Under the Host UM rung sit three struck-through red chips, the reductions that do not apply here (BI OFFSET · MEDPAY NON-DUPLICATION · MEDPAY LIEN), tied by one mono pill: HARBOR POINT LETTER 03/02/2026. A grey chip, NO PIP ON THIS FILE, sits apart. Bottom strip: a cream card, CONFIRM SCOPE · RELEASE · LIENS · PAYEE · CHECK DATE → CONFIRMING EMAIL. The argument: the gross is protected by documents checked before the negotiation.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Tell Them

*Why:* Once Keystone tenders, the money that makes John Doe whole sits with two first-party carriers that insure him, and both will argue against him. $40,000 is reached by proving the gap above each layer, closing every reduction in writing first, and papering every move.

1. UM pays the gap: damages, as Atty. Thorne values them, minus the BI, up to its limit. Relative UM is excess over Host UM, so Cardinal State's file must show damages above $30,000. Never demand a limit without showing the gap.
2. Check the offset rule first: a reduction policy cuts UM by the BI; add-on pays on top. Harbor Point's 03/02/2026 letter answers the limit half of “BI already compensated him.” The damages half needs proof: its full $20,000 needs damages of at least $30,000.
3. These are first-party contract claims, yet each carrier turns adversarial at evaluation. The first offer is rarely the last.
4. Expect value, MMI and lien challenges. Answer value with the MRI, the injection, residuals at MMI (06/12/2026) and future care of $3,400–$6,800. MMI is reached; work the liens now.
5. Bracketing is Atty. Thorne's tool: he sets the range and makes every move; you relay a bracket only on his written, word-for-word instruction. Against a documented gap, he may simply hold.
6. When a carrier offers limits, confirm its terms (coverage, release scope, liens, payee, check date) without accepting; report to Atty. Thorne that day. Only after John Doe decides, on his advice, does the acceptance go out: you draft it, he approves and sends it.
7. A first-party carrier owes its insured good faith. Never accept verbally under pressure: log the words, put new evidence in writing under Atty. Thorne's deadline, escalate. Bad faith is his call.
8. Payout: release → check (to John Doe and the firm; he endorses, never the CM) → IOLTA → liens, fee, costs → net. Log each check the day it arrives; deposit it that day if its release is signed, else secure it and expedite signing under Atty. Thorne.
9. MedPay is not PIP: in most states a collateral source, not a credit to Keystone (confirm the rule). Exhausted and non-subrogated, it creates no lien and no UM cut here. Where PIP applies, set-off, the EMC ceiling and subrogation change the ask.

> ⚠️ **UPL boundary.** You may assemble the evidence, log and relay offers, and paper what John Doe decided through Atty. Thorne. You may not accept, reject or counter, tell John Doe whether to settle, explain a policy's offset or release language to him, or accuse a carrier of bad faith.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Show Them

*What can shrink the gross, and where this file stands*

| Reduction | Where it bites | On this file |
|---|---|---|
| PIP set-off | No-fault states: value – PIP paid = what the defendant still owes. | No PIP. MedPay is not PIP: usually a collateral source, not a credit to Keystone (confirm the rule). Exhausted, non-subrogated: no lien, no UM cut. |
| EMC ceiling | In PIP states, some cap benefits lower without an emergency-medical-condition finding (confirm the rule); this file has no PIP. | MedPay paid its full $5,000.00; the rest of the care became Peach State and LOP liens. |
| Subrogation | A subrogated carrier liens the settlement: log it, plan the reduction. | Harbor Point, 03/02/2026: no reimbursement right. |
| UM offset | A reduction policy subtracts the BI from the UM limit. | Add-on: $20,000 of room, not $20,000 – $10,000 = $10,000. |

**Operational mapping**

| Trigger Event | ➔ Case Manager Task | ➔ Tool/Interface | ➔ Verification Checkpoint |
|---|---|---|---|
| UM offer received (Harbor Point, 09/14/2026) | Log the offer and its stated reasons; report to Atty. Thorne | CMS → Insurance tab → Negotiation Log · CMS → Tasks → Escalate | Offer, date and quoted reasons logged and reported 09/14/2026 |
| Offer cites the BI payment or the gap | Assemble the counter-package for approval: the 03/02/2026 letter, MRI read, Causation Letter, MMI residuals, future care | Action Plan Editor · CMS → Documents → Outgoing | Approved by Atty. Thorne and sent 09/16/2026 |
| Carrier offers its limits | Confirm scope, release, liens, payee and check date without accepting; report to Atty. Thorne; draft the acceptance email | Email Workspace · CMS → Case Notes | Acceptance email sent only after John Doe's decision is logged and Atty. Thorne approves it |
| UM check arrives | Confirm its UM release is signed, then deposit to trust the same day; if not, secure the check and route it to Atty. Thorne | CMS → Financial Ledger → Trust | Each UM release signed before its check; deposited the day received (10/05 and 10/13/2026) |

**Best practices & pitfalls**

- Look for every stacking and resident-relative policy; it is why this file reaches $40,000.
- Confirm offset and reimbursement status in writing from each carrier, as Harbor Point's 03/02/2026 letter does.
- Set John Doe's expectations on the UM timeline (demand, evaluation, possibly an IME, negotiation; usually faster than BI because the records are already collected); never promise him a limit.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: accepting the first offer because “it's his own coverage.”
- ⚠️ Pitfall: promising a full UM limit before the offset rule is checked.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: a bracket move with no reciprocal move from the carrier (a move for Atty. Thorne to make or refuse, never yours).
- ⚠️ Pitfall: a verbal settlement with no confirming email.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: finding a benefit-carrier lien after disbursement.

#### 3 · Knowledge Check & CM Summary

Verified in Negotiation Mastery Check (taught on Case Flow Day 5) and that day's auto-graded questions.

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Gap and layer logic | UM pays the gap above BI; Relative UM excess over Host UM; $10,000 + $20,000 + $10,000 = $40,000 |
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Reductions closed | Offset, MedPay non-duplication and MedPay reimbursement each tied to the 03/02/2026 letter; no PIP on the file; PIP rules stated as where they apply |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Close and paper trail | Coverage, release, liens, payee and check date confirmed; same-day email |
| 🤖 AI | **10%** | Payout sequence | Release → check → trust → liens, fee and costs → net |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **20%** | Strategic judgment | Explains why the gross is protected before the negotiation, and when bracketing does and doesn't fit |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **20%** | UPL boundary | No acceptance, valuation, policy interpretation or bad-faith accusation by the CM; every acceptance John Doe's, through Atty. Thorne |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Open on Harbor Point's 03/02/2026 letter. Ask which sentence in it is worth $10,000 to John Doe. It is the add-on clause: on a reduction policy, Keystone's $10,000 would have come off the top of the $20,000.
- Put to the room: “Harbor Point insures Kevin Alvarez's car. Why does it owe John Doe anything, and why will it still fight him?”
- Watch for trainees who treat the UM carriers as friendly because they are first-party. They turn adversarial at evaluation, and Harbor Point's 09/14 offer proves it. Teach PIP as the doctrine where it applies, not as a fact of this file: where PIP applies, the set-off, the EMC ceiling and a subrogation lien each change the BI ask and the net.
- Bad faith is a legal conclusion. New medical evidence changes a valuation, and on this file it is the residual paresthesia Dr. Reyes documented at MMI; don't call it permanent unless a physician has. The CM's contribution is a dated record of what each carrier knew and said, and anyone who writes “bad faith” to an adjuster has crossed the line.

### 5.1.3 Statutory Liens, Health Subrogation & Provider Balances

> **Lesson** · Day 5 — Settlement, closing & the litigation fork · Module 5: Closing & Settlement · scored with Lien Reduction Verification (`d3m3`)  
> **Topic:** Not all liens are created equal: classify before you negotiate  
> **Continuity:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · Atty. Elijah Thorne · $10,000 BI / $20,000 HOST UM / $10,000 REL UM · 14-Day Gap  
> **Theory extracted from:** `D3#18` Lien Negotiations/Reduction: Common Types of Liens · `D3#19` Key Legal Doctrines Used for Reduction · `D3#20` Quantum Meruit Principle — Prior Attorney Liens · Newly authored: plan funding status (self-funded vs. fully insured) decides the law that governs a health-plan reduction; the verified-none inventory of absent lienholders

#### 1 · Visual Layout Description

A reducibility ladder fills the left two-thirds: horizontal bars stacked from MOST NEGOTIABLE at the top (PROVIDER LOP, navy) through HEALTH PLAN · FULLY INSURED and WORKERS' COMP (navy-soft) and MEDICARE/MEDICAID (grey) to ERISA SELF-FUNDED at the bottom (dark grey). The two live liens, Riverton Spine & Rehab and Peach State, are solid bars with mono pills, $6,290.00 and $1,860.00; every other bar is hollow with a green ✓ NONE — VERIFIED chip. Beside the ladder, an outlined PRIOR ATTORNEY bar tagged QUANTUM MERUIT. Right third: three cream doctrine cards with orange numbered badges, ① COMMON FUND ② MADE WHOLE ③ COMPARATIVE FAULT, the third greyed. Bottom: a red danger strip, “Unclassified = overpaid or frozen.” The argument: absent liens are checked as carefully as present ones.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Tell Them

*Why:* $40,000 is not John Doe's until every claim on it is found, classified and resolved in writing. Liens are not created equal: a statutory lien bends to a formula, a Health Plan's reimbursement right to a doctrine, a provider's balance to a negotiation. Classify one wrong and you overpay it or freeze the disbursement.

1. Statutory liens (Medicare, Medicaid, a statutory hospital lien, workers' comp) arise by law and bend least. Contractual claims (a plan's reimbursement clause, an LOP) rest on an agreement.
2. Inventory every claimant, absent ones included. Two liens are asserted: Peach State and Riverton Spine & Rehab. Record each “none” with its source: Medicare (the Day 3 SSDI/BCRC check), Medicaid (verified separately: no state-agency interest), hospital (EMS transport declined), workers' comp, prior attorney.
3. Not every payer is a lienholder. Harbor Point's MedPay has no reimbursement right (03/02/2026); Crescent Pain & Spine took Peach State's contract rate and cannot balance-bill John Doe.
4. Funding decides the law. A self-funded ERISA plan answers to federal law and its own terms and is very hard to reduce; a fully insured plan is generally subject to state insurance law. Peach State is fully insured: what follows is Atty. Thorne's call.
5. Common fund: a lienholder repaid from a fund the firm's work and advanced costs created shares the cost of recovery. Atty. Thorne asked for the fee share, one-third: $1,860.00 – $620.00 = $1,240.00 (fee and costs would be $1,860.00 × $13,820.08 ÷ $40,000.00 = $642.63).
6. That work was real: the carriers used the 14-Day Gap to call the injury unrelated; the MRI and Dr. Reyes's Causation Letter answered it. Peach State did none of it.
7. Made whole: in many jurisdictions a subrogated insurer takes nothing until the client is fully compensated. John Doe's recovery was capped by coverage; whether he was made whole is Atty. Thorne's analysis.
8. Comparative fault: if the recovery was cut for the client's fault, the lien takes the same cut (20% at fault, 20% off). On Track A no layer took a fault reduction.
9. Quantum meruit: a client may fire a lawyer at any time; the lawyer is then generally owed the reasonable value of the work done, not the contingency fee. Get the time records; strip clerical hours. No prior attorney here: verify and record it.

> ⚠️ **UPL boundary.** You classify, gather the plan documents, audit and draft the request with its math. The doctrine, the governing law and any figure offered or accepted are Atty. Thorne's: no lien is compromised without his express authority.

> ⚠️ **UPL boundary.** You may tell John Doe what each lienholder asserts and where it stands, never that a lien is invalid or unenforceable.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Show Them

*Every claim on John Doe's $40,000, classified*

| Claimant | Basis · reducibility | On this file |
|---|---|---|
| Riverton Spine & Rehab | Contract (LOP) · high | $6,290.00 billed; audit first |
| Peach State Health Plan | Contract, fully insured · moderate | $1,860.00 asserted |
| Medicare · Medicaid | Statute · low, formula-bound | Medicare: none (Day 3 SSDI/BCRC check); Medicaid: none (verify, record) |
| Hospital · workers' comp | Statute | None asserted: verify, record |
| Prior attorney | Quantum meruit | None: verify with John Doe |
| Harbor Point MedPay | Not a lien | No reimbursement right |

**Operational mapping**

| Trigger Event | ➔ Case Manager Task | ➔ Tool/Interface | ➔ Verification Checkpoint |
|---|---|---|---|
| Gross recovery reaches $40,000.00 (10/06/2026) | Run the final inventory, re-verifying the Day 3 lien register, asserted and absent, and classify each as statutory or contractual | CMS → Financial Ledger → Net Sheet → Lien Tracker | Two liens open; Medicare, Medicaid, hospital, workers' comp, prior attorney and MedPay each recorded “none” with a source |
| Health Plan lien classified | Obtain the plan documents and route the funding status to Atty. Thorne | CMS → Tasks → Escalate | Fully insured status on file; the governing-law call recorded as his |
| Atty. Thorne chooses the reduction basis | Draft the request naming the doctrine, with the math | CMS → Documents → Outgoing | Doctrine named; $1,860.00 – $620.00 = $1,240.00 shown; held for his approval |
| Prior-attorney question open | Ask John Doe and check the intake record; record the answer | CMS → Case Notes | “No prior attorney — verified” logged with its date |

**Best practices & pitfalls**

- Work the most negotiable lien first to build momentum, but resolve every one in writing before disbursement.
- Record a verified “none” the way you record a lien: with its source and its date.
- Name the doctrine and show the math in every Reduction Request.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: treating an ERISA plan like a provider LOP, or a fully insured plan like a self-funded one.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: asking for a “courtesy discount” with no legal basis.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: paying a prior attorney a percentage fee he is no longer entitled to.

#### 3 · Knowledge Check & CM Summary

Verified in Lien Reduction Verification (taught on Case Flow Day 5) and that day's auto-graded questions.

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Classification | Each claimant statutory, contractual or not a lien, and ranked by reducibility |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Inventory of absences | Medicare, Medicaid (sourced separately), hospital, workers' comp, prior attorney and MedPay each recorded with a source |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Doctrine accuracy | Common fund, made whole, comparative fault and quantum meruit each stated correctly |
| 🤖 AI | **10%** | Common-fund math | $1,860.00 – $620.00 = $1,240.00 |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **20%** | Governing-law judgment | Sees that the plan's funding status decides the law, and leaves the call to Atty. Thorne |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **20%** | UPL boundary | No doctrine chosen, figure offered or validity opinion given without counsel |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Open with the inventory, not the negotiation: “How many liens are on John Doe's $40,000?” Most trainees say two. The right answer is two asserted and five checked, each recorded with its source.
- Ask the room: “Peach State paid $1,860.00 on a $3,400.00 injection. Who can come after John Doe for the other $1,540.00?” Nobody: Crescent Pain & Spine took the contract rate and cannot balance-bill.
- Watch for trainees arguing made whole or comparative fault on their own. Naming the doctrines is the Case Manager's job; choosing one is Atty. Thorne's. The same goes for “he was riding to work in Kevin Alvarez's carpool, so is it workers' comp?”: that is a coverage question for him; the Case Manager records that no claim was filed and no lien asserted.
- Quantum meruit is taught, not triggered, on this file. Make them say why it still gets a line in the Lien Tracker: a prior attorney found after disbursement is found too late.

### 5.1.4 Pro-Rata Distribution & Medical Bill Reduction

> **Lesson** · Day 5 — Settlement, closing & the litigation fork · Module 5: Closing & Settlement · scored with Lien Reduction Verification (`d3m3`)  
> **Topic:** Audit first, then show the lienholder the math  
> **Continuity:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · Atty. Elijah Thorne · $10,000 BI / $20,000 HOST UM / $10,000 REL UM · 14-Day Gap  
> **Theory extracted from:** `D3#21` Increasing the Client's Net · `D3#22` Auditing the Billing Before Asking for a Discount · `D2#40` Finalized Settlement: Final Lien Mitigation (The “Reduction”) · `D2#35` The Execution: Lien Payoff Letters (The “Verification” Paperwork) · Newly authored: the pro-rata distribution rule worked on the BI-only illustration; the pro-rata argument restated on true facts for a fund that covers every lien

#### 1 · Visual Layout Description

Top half: the pro-rata illustration as one long horizontal bar, the $6,179.92 fund, split 23.35% navy (PEACH STATE · $1,443.15) and 76.65% orange (RIVERTON SPINE & REHAB · $4,736.77). A bracket beneath it shows $7,965.00 of liens overhanging the bar's right end in hatched red. At the far right a small card in red: JOHN DOE NETS $0.00. Bottom half: a five-step strip of orange numbered badges, ① Audit ② Correct ③ Calculate ④ Ask with authority ⑤ Confirm in writing, ending in a navy ledger chip, NET SHEET UPDATED. The argument: the math is the leverage, and the audit comes before the ask.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Tell Them

*Why:* Half the job is getting money from the carriers; the other half is keeping it from the lienholders. Every dollar a lien gives up lands in John Doe's net. An honest audit and the pro-rata math move a provider further than any plea for a favor.

1. Audit before you ask. Compare the records to the bill line by line: corrections first, reductions second. Never negotiate a percentage off an inflated bill.
2. Riverton Spine & Rehab billed 34 lines × $185.00 = $6,290.00. The records show 33 visits (16 + 17); the 05/04/2026 line is a duplicate. You negotiate from $6,105.00.
3. Match each line's code to that day's treatment note. Upcoding, unbundling and duplicate dates get a demand for correction, not a request for a favor. No line may fall between 04/02 and 04/21/2026, the disruption.
4. Pro rata: when a fund cannot pay every lien in full and no statute or agreement sets an order, liens of equal priority share what is left after fee and costs in proportion to their amounts. Medicare, a statutory hospital lien or some Medicaid recoveries may take priority; confirm the governing rule. Here: two contractual claims.
5. Had only the $10,000 BI been recovered: $10,000.00 – $3,333.33 – $486.75 = $6,179.92 against $7,965.00 of liens. Peach State $1,860.00 ÷ $7,965.00 (23.35%) → $1,443.15; Riverton $6,105.00 ÷ $7,965.00 (76.65%) → $4,736.77. Compute from the fraction, not the rounded percentage. John Doe nets nothing.
6. Argue on true facts only. On Track A the fund covers both liens: never tell a provider it would take only a share, or that the client will refuse the settlement. The true case: a coverage-capped recovery, fee and costs paid from it, a certain payment now.
7. Common fund (procurement costs): in many states a lienholder must reduce by its proportional share of the fee and costs that created the fund: the retainer's fee percentage (33⅓% pre-suit, 40% once suit is filed) plus its pro rata share of costs. It binds some and persuades others; confirm the rule. Atty. Thorne argues it.
8. Ask with authority: send the written request Atty. Thorne approved, then call billing with his target (never a stock 50%), asking for payment in full in the interest of global resolution. A counter outside it goes back to him.
9. No Payoff Letter, no payment. A signed letter states the payment is full and final satisfaction and that John Doe will not be balance-billed or sent to collections. Pay from the payoff figure, never a statement.
10. Update the ledger the day a reduction is confirmed and show John Doe the savings: $1,860.00 + $6,105.00 = $7,965.00 audited → $1,240.00 + $4,580.00 = $5,820.00 paid ($2,145.00 negotiated), plus the $185.00 duplicate removed at audit.

> ⚠️ **UPL boundary.** You audit, calculate, draft and, inside Atty. Thorne's written authority, make the ask. You never offer or accept a figure outside it, never promise a provider payment, and never tell John Doe a bill is invalid. A reduction is final only on his approval and a signed Payoff Letter.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Show Them

*The pro-rata illustration: BI only, $6,179.92 left after fee and costs*

| Lienholder | Audited lien | Share (lien ÷ $7,965.00) | Paid pro rata |
|---|---|---|---|
| Peach State Health Plan | $1,860.00 | 23.35% | $1,443.15 |
| Riverton Spine & Rehab | $6,105.00 | 76.65% | $4,736.77 |
| Total | $7,965.00 | 100% | $6,179.92 |
| John Doe's net | — | — | $0.00 |

**Operational mapping**

| Trigger Event | ➔ Case Manager Task | ➔ Tool/Interface | ➔ Verification Checkpoint |
|---|---|---|---|
| Provider's Final Ledger received | Audit each line against the visit records; request a written correction of any error | Medical Records Requests · CMS → Financial Ledger → Net Sheet → Lien Tracker | 33 visits matched; 05/04/2026 duplicate removed; $6,105.00 |
| Audited balance confirmed | Draft the Reduction Request with the math and obtain Atty. Thorne's written authority | CMS → Documents → Outgoing · CMS → Tasks → Escalate | Authority logged before the request goes or any call is made |
| Reduction agreed | Obtain the signed Payoff Letter; update the Lien Tracker and the Net Sheet | CMS → Financial Ledger → Net Sheet | Full-and-final language on file; net recomputed the same day |

**Best practices & pitfalls**

- Corrections first, reductions second.
- Transparency about savings builds trust: show John Doe his net rising.
- Get every reduction in writing before disbursement.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: negotiating a percentage off an inflated bill.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: agreeing to a reduction verbally with no written confirmation.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: paying from a statement instead of the final payoff figure, or without the full-and-final language.

#### 3 · Knowledge Check & CM Summary

Verified in Lien Reduction Verification (taught on Case Flow Day 5) and that day's auto-graded questions.

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Audit accuracy | 34 lines against 33 visits; the 05/04/2026 duplicate; $6,105.00 |
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Pro-rata math | Shares computed from each lien ÷ $7,965.00; $1,443.15 + $4,736.77 = $6,179.92 |
| 🤖 AI | **10%** | Payoff-letter terms | Full and final satisfaction; no balance billing or collections |
| 🤖 AI | **10%** | Ledger update | $7,965.00 audited → $5,820.00 paid ($2,145.00 negotiated); net $20,359.92 |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **20%** | Negotiation ethics | Argument made on true facts; no bluff about the fund or the client's intentions |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **20%** | Authority boundary | Every ask inside Atty. Thorne's written authority; nothing final without him |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Open with the BI-only table and cover the last row. Ask: “What does John Doe take home?” Then uncover $0.00. That is why this lesson exists.
- Ask the room: “Riverton billed $6,290.00. What number do you negotiate from?” Anyone who says $6,290.00 has skipped the audit.
- Watch for the old script, “reduce or the client refuses the settlement.” It is a threat, and on Track A it is false: all three layers have tendered their limits (Cardinal State's check arrives 10/13), and after fee and costs $40,000.00 – $13,333.33 – $486.75 = $26,179.92 remains against $7,965.00 of liens. Make them rewrite it on true facts: a coverage-capped recovery, the audited balance, a certain payment now.
- The legal nuance: the common-fund rule binds some lienholders by statute or case law and only persuades others, and an LOP provider is often outside it. Which it is here is Atty. Thorne's call; confirm the governing rule.

### 5.1.5 The Dual-Tab Net Sheet: Recovery Ledger + Lien Tracker

> **Lesson** · Day 5 — Settlement, closing & the litigation fork · Module 5: Closing & Settlement · scored with Final Accounting Check (`d3m4`)  
> **Topic:** One Net Sheet, two streams, every dollar's origin visible  
> **Continuity:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · Atty. Elijah Thorne · $10,000 BI / $20,000 HOST UM / $10,000 REL UM · 14-Day Gap  
> **Theory extracted from:** `D2#35` The Execution: Lien Payoff Letters (The “Verification” Paperwork) · `D2#36` The Execution: The Settlement Statement (The “Net” Sheet) · `D3#23` The Net Sheet: Purpose of a Combined BI/UM Net Sheet · `D3#24` The Net Sheet: Stream 1 (BI) and Stream 2 (UM/UIM) · `D3#25` The Net Sheet: The LSH Corporate Net Sheet Ledger · `D3#26` The Net Sheet: Case Manager Protocols for Dual Settlements · `D3#27` Key Takeaway — The Value of a Case Manager

#### 1 · Visual Layout Description

A wide two-tab spreadsheet card fills the slide, cream with a navy header bar carrying two tabs: RECOVERY LEDGER (active, orange underline) and LIEN TRACKER (navy-soft, set behind). The ledger has three mono columns, TOTAL · STREAM 1 BI · STREAM 2 UM, and rows Gross, Fee 33⅓%, Costs, Peach State, Riverton Spine & Rehab, closing on a heavy orange-ruled NET row: $20,359.92 = $5,089.98 + $15,269.94. A thin orange connector runs from each lien row back to the Lien Tracker tab, labelled PROVED BY PAYOFF LETTER. Bottom-right, a red danger callout: “BI alone: $0.00 to John Doe.” The argument: every figure on the net row traces to its source.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Tell Them

*Why:* John Doe's $40,000 came from three carriers in two streams and goes out as five checks. The Net Sheet shows every dollar's origin and destination before a cent leaves trust. Had only the $10,000 BI been recovered, $6,179.92 would have met $7,965.00 of unreduced liens, and John would have received nothing.

1. One Net Sheet, two tabs. The Recovery Ledger carries the money in and every deduction; the Lien Tracker proves each lien figure the ledger uses. Neither is final until the two tie out.
2. Keep the streams apart. Stream 1, third-party BI: Keystone, $10,000.00. Stream 2, first-party UM: Harbor Point $20,000.00 + Cardinal State (resident relative) $10,000.00 = $30,000.00. Stream 2 opened only because BI was exhausted first.
3. Show where every dollar came from: carriers audit settlements, and a payment counted twice is double-counting on the ledger. Harbor Point's MedPay already paid $5,000.00 of the bills: a payment source on the Lien Tracker, never recovery.
4. Price the fee per stream. The retainer sets 33⅓% pre-suit: $3,333.33 on BI + $10,000.00 on UM = $13,333.33. Separate lines matter: fee terms can differ between BI and UM, and the 40% tier applies only once suit is filed.
5. Every cost needs a receipt: Police Report $25.00, records and billing $214.75, certified mail $72.00, MRI disc $25.00, postage and copies $150.00 = $486.75. Charge it once, as one line or split pro rata; this file splits 25% / 75%: $121.69 / $365.06.
6. Reduce liens globally against the combined $40,000.00, not provider by provider per policy, then allocate pro rata: Peach State $310.00 / $930.00; Riverton Spine & Rehab $1,145.00 / $3,435.00.
7. On the Lien Tracker, Balance Owed = Charges – PIP – Health – MedPay – Adjustments. Each lien shows asserted, audited and reduced amounts and its payoff-letter status. No Payoff Letter, no payment.
8. Run the aggregate math on both streams at once: $5,089.98 + $15,269.94 = $20,359.92, and Gross – Fees & Costs – Final Medical Balances: $40,000.00 – $13,820.08 – $5,820.00 = $20,359.92.
9. Test the net against the future care discussed in treatment: possible repeat ESIs ($3,400–$6,800). The settlement is full and final, so no carrier pays more for them. Flag it for Atty. Thorne.

> ⚠️ **UPL boundary.** You build, verify and explain the arithmetic. You may not tell John Doe whether the net is enough, whether a lien is enforceable, or what he should do with the money: Atty. Thorne reviews the Net Sheet and explains it to John before he signs.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Show Them

*The two tabs of John Doe's Net Sheet, and what each proves*

| RECOVERY LEDGER — money in, deductions, net | LIEN TRACKER — every figure proved |
|---|---|
| Gross $40,000.00: Stream 1 BI $10,000.00 (Keystone) · Stream 2 UM $30,000.00 (Harbor Point + Cardinal State). | Peach State: asserted $1,860.00 → reduced $1,240.00 (10/09). |
| Fee 33⅓%: $3,333.33 + $10,000.00 = $13,333.33. | Riverton Spine & Rehab: $6,290.00 → audited $6,105.00 → reduced $4,580.00 (10/12). |
| Costs, receipted: $121.69 + $365.06 = $486.75. | Harbor Point MedPay $5,000.00: no reimbursement right (03/02/2026) → $0.00. |
| Liens from the Lien Tracker, 25% / 75%: $1,455.00 + $4,365.00 = $5,820.00. | Crescent Pain & Spine: balance written off, no balance-billing → $0.00. |
| Net to John Doe: $5,089.98 + $15,269.94 = $20,359.92. | Payoff Letter status on every line. |

**Operational mapping**

| Trigger Event | ➔ Case Manager Task | ➔ Tool/Interface | ➔ Verification Checkpoint |
|---|---|---|---|
| Each settlement check received | Post it to its stream with carrier and check reference | CMS → Financial Ledger → Net Sheet → Recovery Ledger | BI $10,000.00 · UM $20,000.00 + $10,000.00 · total $40,000.00 |
| Cost incurred | Attach the receipt and post it once | CMS → Financial Ledger → Net Sheet → Recovery Ledger | Five receipts = $486.75; no cost charged to both streams in full |
| Lien reduction accepted | Record asserted, audited and reduced figures and the Payoff Letter | CMS → Financial Ledger → Net Sheet → Lien Tracker | Peach State $1,240.00 · Riverton Spine & Rehab $4,580.00 · letters dated |
| Both tabs complete | Tie the Net Sheet to the Trust Ledger and route it to Atty. Thorne | CMS → Financial Ledger → Trust · CMS → Tasks → Escalate | Net $20,359.92 on both; routed before John Doe sees it |

**Best practices & pitfalls**

- One Net Sheet, two clearly separated streams.
- Every reduction carries its reason: an audit finding (the 05/04/2026 duplicate) or a named basis (the one-third procurement-cost reduction).
- Show the streams side by side so John Doe sees what the work produced: Keystone offered $3,500 on the 14-Day Gap and a “degenerative” MRI; the MRI's own “no significant degenerative change at the adjacent levels” helped turn that into $10,000, and the UM stream added $30,000. Financial engineering, not data entry.
- Read the LSH ledger top to bottom in four sections: recovery summary (gross by carrier), fees and advanced costs, the medical provider ledger with its reductions, net to client.
- Accurate financial detail builds client trust.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: mixing BI and UM money on one undifferentiated line.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: charging the same cost to both streams.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: a cost with no receipt, or a lien figure with no Payoff Letter.

#### 3 · Knowledge Check & CM Summary

Verified in Final Accounting Check (taught on Case Flow Day 5) and that day's auto-graded questions.

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Stream separation | BI $10,000.00 and UM $30,000.00 on separate streams; MedPay kept out of the gross |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Fee and cost lines | $3,333.33 + $10,000.00 fee; five receipted costs = $486.75, split $121.69 / $365.06, charged once |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Lien Tracker fields | Asserted, audited, reduced and payoff status for each lien; Balance Owed formula applied |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Aggregate math | $20,359.92 reached both down the streams and across the total |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **15%** | Double-counting reasoning | Explains why MedPay and written-off balances never appear as payables |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **10%** | Future-care judgment | Recognises the full-and-final consequence and flags it rather than resolving it |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **15%** | UPL boundary | Explains the arithmetic to John Doe; leaves sufficiency, enforceability and advice to Atty. Thorne |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Open with the why's counterfactual: write $6,179.92 against $7,965.00 on the board and ask what John Doe would have taken home with the BI stream alone. Nothing. Then show $20,359.92.
- Ask the room: “Why does Harbor Point's MedPay appear on the Lien Tracker but not in the gross?” The answer is double-counting on the ledger: it already paid $5,000.00 of the bills, and it has no reimbursement right on this file.
- Watch the rounding. 25% of $486.75 is 121.6875, so BI carries $121.69 and UM takes the remainder, $365.06. Compute the second stream as the remainder and the total never drifts by a cent.
- Legal nuance: Peach State is a fully insured plan, so its reduction turned on state law, not ERISA preemption. That was Atty. Thorne's call in the lien negotiation; the CM records the basis and never chooses it.

### 5.1.6 Release Audits, Trust Accounting & Disbursement Commands

> **Lesson** · Day 5 — Settlement, closing & the litigation fork · Module 5: Closing & Settlement · scored with Final Accounting Check (`d3m4`)  
> **Topic:** No money moves until every gate behind it is closed  
> **Continuity:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · Atty. Elijah Thorne · $10,000 BI / $20,000 HOST UM / $10,000 REL UM · 14-Day Gap  
> **Theory extracted from:** `D2#33` The Execution: The Release of All Claims (The “Exit” Document) · `D2#34` The Execution: Settlement Disclosure Statement (The “Truth” Document) · `D2#35` The Execution: Lien Payoff Letters (The “Verification” Paperwork) · `D2#36` The Execution: The Settlement Statement (The “Net” Sheet) · `D2#37` The Execution: The Dismissal (The “Court” Filing) · `D2#38` The Execution: W-9 & Comparison of Key Post-Settlement Documents · `D2#39` Finalized Settlement: Boots-on-the-Ground Tasks — Administrative Document Control · `D3#26` The Net Sheet: Case Manager Protocols for Dual Settlements · `D3#29` Case Phase: Disbursement — Core Objectives · `D3#30` Disbursement: The Workflow · `D3#32` Disbursement: Common Challenges & Mitigation · Newly authored: trust-account handling (same-day IOLTA deposit once the release is signed, client endorsement, no disbursement against uncleared funds, disputed shares held in trust); the contingent-fee Settlement Statement (ABA Model Rule 1.5(c) as an example); the UM release's Medicare and confidentiality terms

#### 1 · Visual Layout Description

A horizontal six-gate money path across the slide, each gate a navy card with an orange numbered badge and a mono date pill: ① Releases signed · 08/28 + UM ② Checks into IOLTA · 09/04 · 10/05 · 10/13 ③ Funds clear · 10/20 ④ Statement signed · 10/21 ⑤ Atty. Thorne signs · 10/22 ⑥ Reconciled · $0.00. Above gate ①, a red danger callout quotes Keystone's 08/14 draft, “all other persons, firms, corporations and insurers”, struck through, with –$30,000 beside it. Below the path, a slim grey trust-ledger bar steps from $40,000.00 down to $0.00 in mono. The argument: money moves only after every gate to its left is closed.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Tell Them

*Why:* A settlement is not over when a carrier says yes. It is over when the right release is signed, every check has cleared trust, every lien is paid off in writing and the ledger reads $0.00. One unread line in Keystone's 08/14 draft would have released the UM carriers and cost John Doe $30,000.

1. Read every release line by line before John Doe sees it: who is released, which claims, and what John promises. Keystone's 08/14 draft released “all other persons, firms, corporations and insurers”: Harbor Point and Cardinal State. Flagged, it became the Limited Release John signed 08/28.
2. A BI release must not reach what is still open: the Civic's property-damage claim runs separately with Keystone PD. Both UM consents come before the BI release is signed: here 08/24 and 08/26, before 08/28.
3. Non-admission language is standard: Keystone pays to end the claim, not to admit fault. The indemnity and hold-harmless clause binds John Doe to repay the carrier if a lienholder later pursues it, so every lien must be on the Lien Tracker and paid off in writing.
4. Audit UM releases scope first: Harbor Point's, signed while Cardinal State's claim is open, releases Harbor Point only (“all other … insurers” would reach Cardinal State's $10,000); neither reaches the PD claim. Then indemnity, confidentiality, Medicare (John Doe is no beneficiary: verify it). Terms: Atty. Thorne's call.
5. The Settlement Statement shows John Doe how $40,000.00 becomes $20,359.92, and that it is full and final. Contingent-fee rules commonly require one (for example, ABA Model Rule 1.5(c); confirm the governing rule). Where a cooling-off period applies, no check is cut before it ends.
6. Log each check on arrival; deposit it to IOLTA that day once its release is signed (else secure it for Atty. Thorne), never to operating. John Doe endorses, never the CM. Nothing is disbursed against uncleared funds (here, before 10/20); a disputed share stays in trust. Confirm the governing trust rule.
7. Disbursement serves accuracy, compliance and fraud prevention: Verification (conditions precedent) → Documentation (Payoff Letters, receipts with tax IDs, W-9s) → Authorization (Atty. Thorne signs) → Reconciliation ($0.00).
8. No suit was filed on Track A, so no dismissal is needed. Where a suit is pending, settlement is not over until the court is told: prepare the stipulation of dismissal with prejudice for the attorneys' signatures, docket it and confirm the entry.

> ⚠️ **UPL boundary.** You audit, flag, prepare and verify. You never explain what a release clause means for John Doe, agree wording with an adjuster, or sign or release a trust check: Atty. Thorne settles every term, explains the release and the statement to John, and approves and signs every disbursement.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Show Them

*The money path on John Doe's file: no gate opens before the one to its left has closed*

1. **Releases signed** — Keystone's Limited Release 08/28 (08/14 draft flagged); each UM release before its check.
2. **Checks into trust** — IOLTA deposits 09/04 · 10/05 · 10/13 = $40,000.00.
3. **Funds clear** — 10/20. Nothing is disbursed before it.
4. **Statement signed** — John Doe, 10/21, after Atty. Thorne explains it.
5. **Disbursement** — 10/22: five checks, approved and signed by Atty. Thorne.
6. **Reconcile** — Ledger $0.00; lien satisfactions and John Doe's receipt filed; closing memo 10/30.

**Operational mapping**

| Trigger Event | ➔ Case Manager Task | ➔ Tool/Interface | ➔ Verification Checkpoint |
|---|---|---|---|
| Settlement agreed or tendered | Request the payee's W-9 the same day and send it with the signed release | CMS → Documents → Outgoing | W-9 travels with the release; no accounting hold on the check |
| Carrier's draft release arrives | Audit parties, claims, indemnity, Medicare and confidentiality terms; route a redline memo | CMS → Documents → Release audit · CMS → Tasks → Escalate | Every over-broad line quoted and flagged before John Doe sees the draft |
| Settlement check received | Confirm its release is signed, then deposit to IOLTA the same day and log the clearance date | CMS → Financial Ledger → Trust | Deposits 09/04, 10/05, 10/13 = $40,000.00; all funds cleared by 10/20 |
| Suit pending at settlement (not on Track A) | Prepare the stipulation of dismissal with prejudice for the attorneys' signatures | Court E-Filing · CMS → Calendar | “With prejudice” verified; court entry confirmed and docketed |

**Best practices & pitfalls**

- Match each post-settlement document to its signer: release, John Doe; Payoff Letter, the lienholder; W-9, the payee; stipulation of dismissal, the attorneys.
- Send the release by secure e-signature or schedule an in-person signing; if a notary is required, check the stamp is current and the ID matches “John Doe” exactly.
- Kickback Protocol: when paperwork is missing, say exactly what is missing and give a deadline. A change of circumstance (new address, new payee name) needs updated documents signed by John Doe.
- Cross-reference invoice numbers before any payment is approved: the 05/04/2026 duplicate on Riverton Spine & Rehab's bill is why.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: a General Release that wipes out the UM claims or the open property-damage claim.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: cutting checks against uncleared funds, or during a cooling-off period.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: paying from emailed payment instructions without verifying them by phone to a number on file.

#### 3 · Knowledge Check & CM Summary

Verified in Final Accounting Check (taught on Case Flow Day 5) and that day's auto-graded questions.

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Release audit points | Parties, claims released, indemnity, non-admission, Medicare and confidentiality each named; the 08/14 catch-all line identified |
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Trust sequence | Each release signed before its deposit; deposits 09/04 · 10/05 · 10/13; all cleared by 10/20; signed 10/21; disbursed 10/22; ledger $0.00 |
| 🤖 AI | **10%** | Disbursement workflow | Verification → Documentation → Authorization → Reconciliation, in order |
| 🤖 AI | **10%** | Dismissal rule | None on Track A; with prejudice, signed by the attorneys and entry confirmed where a suit is pending |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **15%** | Risk reasoning | Explains what each over-broad clause would cost John Doe, in dollars and claims |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **10%** | Compliance judgment | Treats cleared funds and the signed statement as hard gates, not formalities |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **15%** | UPL boundary | Flags and routes release terms; never interprets them for John Doe or agrees them with an adjuster |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Open with the struck-through line from Keystone's 08/14 draft and ask what it would have cost. The answer, $30,000, is the whole case for auditing every release.
- Put to the room: “Keystone's check is in. Why can't we pay Riverton Spine & Rehab today?” Make them name every gate: cleared funds, the signed statement, the Payoff Letter, Atty. Thorne's signature.
- Watch for trainees who read the indemnity clause as boilerplate. Tie it back to the Lien Tracker: it is John Doe's promise that every lien will be paid, and the CM's records are what make the promise safe.
- Track A has no lawsuit, so no dismissal. Teach it anyway: a file settled after suit is filed is not closed until the court enters the dismissal with prejudice, and a forgotten dismissal leaves a live case on the docket.

### 5.1.7 Impasse Triggers & Filing Criteria

> **Lesson** · Day 5 — Settlement, closing & the litigation fork · Module 5: Closing & Settlement (condensed) · scored with Litigation Fork Transition Check (`d4m1`)  
> **Topic:** Where Track B leaves Track A, and when a stall becomes an impasse  
> **Continuity:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · Atty. Elijah Thorne · $10,000 BI / $20,000 HOST UM / $10,000 REL UM · 14-Day Gap  
> **Theory extracted from:** `D4#1` Welcome to Day 4: When Pre-Suit Negotiations Stall · `D2#29` Settlement Negotiations: The “Never” Rule · LSH Claims Specialist: liability denial and disputes — the dispute path to litigation; duty, breach, causation and damages; ask for the denial's reasons and evidence in writing · Newly authored: the five impasse triggers, each proved from the file

#### 1 · Visual Layout Description

A fork diagram fills the upper two-thirds. From the left, a single navy trunk labeled SAME FILE · 08/05/2026 · $3,500 OFFER runs to a split point. The upper branch, grayed back, reads TRACK A · DAY 3 — TENDER → UM OPENS. The lower branch, in orange, carries six mono-pill stations — 08/12 “FINAL” · 08/21 LAPSE · 08/24 · 08/31 · 09/02 ESCALATION · 09/04 SILENCE — ending at a navy card: IMPASSE MEMO 09/08 → ATTY. THORNE DECIDES. Below, three cream stack rungs ($10,000 · $20,000 · $10,000) sit behind a red padlock on the BI rung, captioned “$40,000 frozen behind a $10,000 carrier.” The padlock is the argument.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Tell Them

*Why:* Track B is the same file from 08/05/2026, but Keystone won't move off the 14-Day Gap. Because BI must be exhausted before UM opens, one $10,000 carrier now holds the whole $40,000 stack. The Case Manager is the engine room: prove the impasse with dates and put a decision-ready record in front of Atty. Thorne.

1. Track B is the same file, back at 08/05/2026. On Track A Keystone tendered and the file settled. Here Keystone holds at $3,500, calls it “final” on 08/12 and lets the demand lapse 08/21.
2. When pre-suit negotiation stalls, the file moves to formal dispute resolution: litigation, mediation (non-binding; attendance may be ordered, agreement never is) or arbitration (trial-like, usually binding). ADR is often faster, more private and cheaper.
3. Work the dispute path first: evidence assembled, negotiation re-engaged with new findings, the adjuster's supervisor escalated. Litigation comes only when none of it moves the carrier.
4. Document impasse on its triggers, not on frustration (the table below); whether the file is at impasse is Atty. Thorne's call. By 09/04/2026 all five are on this file.
5. Name the carrier's ground. Keystone's $3,500 rests on the 14-Day Gap and a “degenerative” MRI; the 08/06 rebuttal answered both; the 08/12 “final” answered none of it.
6. Hold the line while you test it (the “Never” rule): never bid against yourself, never apologize for a Limits Demand, never accept “that's all I have” as final. Every adjuster has a supervisor; the 09/02 escalation was that test. Any new number is Atty. Thorne's.
7. Know what a BI impasse costs. As this file's policies are configured, Host UM pays only after BI is exhausted (paid or tendered at limits), Relative UM behind it: $40,000 frozen behind a $10,000 carrier. Exhaustion rules vary by policy and jurisdiction; Atty. Thorne confirms the governing rule.
8. Filing is counsel's decision; its inputs are yours to verify. Liability: RPD-26-018842, Ruth Calloway, the doorbell video. Causation: the MRI, the Causation Letter, the EMS report. Damages: $16,401.40 in Specials. Time: SOL 02/06/2028, internal cutoff 11/05/2027.
9. Record the lapse; don't interpret it. Proof of delivery (07/22) and the 08/21 lapse may bear on Robert Smith's exposure above his limits: Atty. Thorne's analysis, never yours to raise with Keystone or John Doe.

> ⚠️ **UPL boundary.** You may document the impasse, report dates and figures, and ask Atty. Thorne for a decision. You may not recommend suit, value the claim, assess Robert Smith's exposure above his limits, or tell John Doe whether to take $3,500.

> ⚠️ **UPL boundary.** If Keystone calls with a new number, take it down, confirm nothing, and route it to Atty. Thorne the same day. Every letter to Keystone after 08/21/2026 uses wording he approved; it can affect the lapse he is analyzing.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Show Them

*The five impasse triggers, and where each sits on John Doe's file*

| Trigger | On this file | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| Same offer, no new reason | $3,500 on 08/05; “final” on 08/12 | Communications Log |
| Objection kept after rebuttal | The gap and a “degenerative” MRI, answered 08/06 | Rebuttal and delivery proof |
| Demand deadline passed | Time-Limited Demand lapsed 08/21/2026 | Return receipt (07/22); calendar |
| Follow-ups unanswered | 08/24 and 08/31 | Sent emails; log |
| Escalation ignored | Claims supervisor 09/02; nothing by 09/04 | Escalation email; log |

**Operational mapping**

| Trigger Event | ➔ Case Manager Task | ➔ Tool/Interface | ➔ Verification Checkpoint |
|---|---|---|---|
| Offer repeated or a demand deadline passes unaccepted | Log the trigger with its date and proof | CMS → Communications Log | Every trigger dated and tied to a document |
| Follow-ups unanswered | Escalate to the adjuster's supervisor; send no new number | Email Workspace · CMS → Communications Log | Escalation dated; asks for Keystone's written position and evidence; restates no offer and reopens no deadline unless Atty. Thorne approved the wording |
| Escalation window closes unanswered | Assemble the impasse record and the filing inputs for counsel | CMS → Tasks → Escalate | Routed to Atty. Thorne with the SOL and internal dates |

**Best practices & pitfalls**

- Date every contact the day it happens; an impasse is proved by the record, not by memory.
- Ask the carrier for its reasons and the evidence it relies on, in writing. A position you can quote is one counsel can use.
- Escalate a silent adjuster to the supervisor before you call impasse.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: treating silence as negotiation and waiting another month.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: sending a UM demand before BI is exhausted. The UM claims are open and noticed; only the demand waits.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: telling John Doe the case “will have to go to court.”

#### 3 · Knowledge Check & CM Summary

Verified in Litigation Fork Transition Check (taught on Case Flow Day 5) and that day's auto-graded questions.

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Trigger identification | All five impasse triggers named, each with its date between 08/05 and 09/04/2026 |
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Frozen stack | BI → Host UM → Relative UM; $30,000 of UM closed until BI is exhausted; $40,000 total |
| 🤖 AI | **10%** | Filing inputs | Liability, causation, damages and time each tied to a document on the file |
| 🤖 AI | **10%** | Clock | SOL 02/06/2028 and internal cutoff 11/05/2027 stated correctly |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **15%** | “Never” rule judgment | Holds the demand without bidding against itself, and escalates instead of accepting “final” |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **25%** | UPL boundary | Documents and asks: no recommendation to sue, no value, no excess-exposure opinion, nothing to John Doe on the offer |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Open with the fork itself: “Same file, same 08/05/2026 offer. On Track A Keystone tendered. Here it doesn't.” Then point at the padlock: one carrier with a $10,000 limit is holding $40,000.
- Ask the room: “Keystone hasn't said no. It has said nothing. When does silence become an impasse, and who decides that it has?” Expect “the Case Manager”; correct it. The Case Manager documents; Atty. Thorne decides.
- Watch for trainees who want to “just demand the UM limits” while BI stalls. It is Day 2's order-of-operations error in new clothes: a UM demand before BI is exhausted risks the stack.
- The legal nuance: the 08/21 lapse may matter to Robert Smith's exposure above his limits. Say that it may, and that it is Atty. Thorne's analysis. A trainee who writes it into a memo or says it to John Doe has crossed the line.

### 5.1.8 From Pre-Lit to Litigation & ADR

> **Lesson** · Day 5 — Settlement, closing & the litigation fork · Module 5: Closing & Settlement (condensed) · scored with Litigation Fork Transition Check (`d4m1`)  
> **Topic:** Status, court fields, litigation folders, the Litigation Calendar and the neutral  
> **Continuity:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · Atty. Elijah Thorne · $10,000 BI / $20,000 HOST UM / $10,000 REL UM · 14-Day Gap  
> **Theory extracted from:** `D5#31` Litigation File Architecture · `D4#1` Welcome to Day 4: When Pre-Suit Negotiations Stall · `D4#2` Introducing the Neutral: Mediator vs. Arbitrator — Expertise & Background · `D4#3` Introducing the Neutral: Assignment & Selection Workflows · `D4#4` Introducing the Neutral: Extent of Decision-Making Power · `D4#31` Case Manager Operational Countermeasures: The 48-Hour Mandate · LSH Claims Specialist: Letter for Preservation of Evidence · Newly authored: a UM notice is not a demand; status, fields, folders and calendar changed together

#### 1 · Visual Layout Description

A before-and-after split. Left panel, muted gray, titled PRE-LIT FILE: a claim folder, an adjuster-response clock and a 33⅓% chip. A thick orange → crosses the center, labeled STATUS → LITIGATION · ON COUNSEL'S AUTHORITY. Right panel, crisp navy on white, titled LITIGATION / ADR FILE: a directory tree with five numbered mono-pill sub-folders 01–05; a court-fields card with CIVIL ACTION NO. as a dashed PENDING pill; a calendar strip with a small 48-HR AUDIT flag before each hard date; a 40% chip tagged ON FILING. Beneath, two neutral cards: MEDIATOR · NO POWER TO DECIDE in cream, ARBITRATOR · BINDING in navy with a red FINAL seal.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Tell Them

*Why:* Once Atty. Thorne authorizes suit, a court clock replaces the adjuster's. Status, court fields, folders and calendar change together, or a pre-lit file keeps pre-lit habits while court deadlines run. Later the file meets two neutrals with very different power.

1. Status changes to Litigation only on Atty. Thorne's dated authorization to sue, and cites it. The fee tier follows the filing, not the status: 33⅓% pre-suit, 40% once suit is filed, per the retainer.
2. Open the court fields and fill them only from the record: court, civil action number, filing date, defense counsel, service status. Until the Complaint is filed the number reads “Pending”; never guess one.
3. Build the litigation directory at transition, so it is ready the moment the Complaint is filed (09/24/2026): DOE, JOHN - CASE FILE with 01_PLEADINGS, 02_SERVICE_DOCS, 03_DISCOVERY_PLAINTIFF_TO_DEFENDANT, 04_DISCOVERY_DEFENDANT_TO_PLAINTIFF, 05_SUBPOENAS.
4. Name every PDF to the convention (Complaint_Filed_Stamped.pdf, never scan001.pdf). File each incoming litigation document within 48 hours, but docket its deadlines and alert Atty. Thorne the day it arrives (an RFA, the hour it arrives).
5. Seed the Litigation Calendar the day status changes: counsel's filing target, SOL 02/06/2028 (internal hard date 02/04/2028, cutoff 11/05/2027), 48-hour audits before hard dates. Ask Atty. Thorne each UM policy's suit or arbitration deadline and any duty to notify the carrier of suit; docket only from his answer.
6. Notify the UM carriers as their policies require and Atty. Thorne directs: the impasse and the intended suit, in writing, with proof of delivery. A notice is not a UM demand and carries no figure.
7. Preserve before you plead. Keep the doorbell video copy unaltered; what others hold (Margaret Doe's camera account, the Escape's event data recorder) gets a written preservation request Atty. Thorne signs.
8. Binding means final. A binding award generally can't be appealed on the merits; courts vacate or correct one only on narrow grounds (fraud, partiality, misconduct, an evident miscalculation), on short deadlines. Confirm the policy and the governing statute.

> ⚠️ **UPL boundary.** You may change fields, build folders, calendar dates, and draft notices and preservation letters for Atty. Thorne's approval. You may tell John Doe what happens next procedurally (dates, logistics, what to bring). What it means for his claim, his fee or the outcome is Atty. Thorne's to explain. Send no notice he has not approved. At mediation or arbitration you run logistics and exhibits; you never speak for John Doe, argue or negotiate.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Show Them

*The two neutrals this file will meet later, and how they differ*

|  | Mediator | Arbitrator |
|---|---|---|
| Background | Retired judge or PI trial lawyer: negotiation, liens, valuation | Retired judge or senior trial lawyer: evidence, liability, causation |
| How chosen | Mutual pick from panel resumes or the court roster | By the policy or a stipulation; often an AAA/JAMS strike list |
| Power | None: cannot force a deal, rule or order payment | Private judge: binding award, no appeal on the merits |
| Binder emphasis | Valuation and settlement history | Admissible evidence, right the first time |
| On this file, later | Hon. Carla Benton (Ret.), court-ordered, 03/18/2027 | Laura Whitcomb, Esq., UM claims, 06/22/2027 |

**Operational mapping**

| Trigger Event | ➔ Case Manager Task | ➔ Tool/Interface | ➔ Verification Checkpoint |
|---|---|---|---|
| Atty. Thorne authorizes suit | Change status and open the court fields | CMS → Case Summary · CMS → Litigation | Status change cites the dated authorization; case number “Pending” |
| Status changed | Build the master directory and the five sub-folders | CMS → Documents → Litigation folders | All five sub-folders, named to the standard |
| Folders built | Seed the Litigation Calendar with 48-hour audits | CMS → Calendar · Docket System | SOL, internal hard date, cutoff and audits all on the calendar |
| A panel list or proposed neutral arrives | Pull resumes and prior-award history for Atty. Thorne; docket any strike deadline | CMS → Litigation → ADR · CMS → Calendar | Deadline docketed the day the list arrives; strikes approved by counsel |

**Best practices & pitfalls**

- Change status, fields, folders and calendar in one sitting, as soon as the authorization is on file.
- Audit, don't just track: in litigation and ADR a missed deadline or a grayscale exhibit can get evidence excluded or cost leverage.
- Tailor the binder to the neutral: valuation for a mediator, evidence for an arbitrator.
- Expectations are Atty. Thorne's to set: mediation can end without a deal; arbitration ends in a binding number. You tell John Doe what happens next procedurally, never what it means for his claim, fee or outcome.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: “scan001.pdf” in a litigation folder.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: treating mediation and arbitration as the same thing.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: missing a strike deadline and accepting a default appointment.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: assuming an arbitration error can be appealed.

#### 3 · Knowledge Check & CM Summary

Verified in Litigation Fork Transition Check (taught on Case Flow Day 5) and that day's auto-graded questions.

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Folder structure | Master directory and all five sub-folders named exactly |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Field discipline | Status change cites the dated authorization; court set; case number “Pending”, not guessed |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Calendar | SOL, internal hard date, cutoff and 48-hour audits seeded |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Neutral distinctions | Mediator vs arbitrator: background, selection and power each stated correctly |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **20%** | Transition judgment | Changes made together, on counsel's authority; fee tier tied to filing; pre-suit record preserved |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **20%** | UPL boundary | No fee or suit explanation to John Doe; no unapproved notice; no advocacy at ADR |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Open on the dashed PENDING pill. Ask what goes in the case-number field on 09/10/2026. Anything but “Pending” is a guess, and a guessed number ends up on a pleading.
- Ask: “Which moves John Doe to the 40% tier: the status change or the filing?” The retainer says filing. A trainee who records 40% from the status date has changed his fee two weeks early.
- Spend real time on the neutrals; trainees conflate them. Put the legacy question: can Atty. Thorne appeal an award that is short because of a math error? No appeal on the merits; at most a narrow motion to correct an evident miscalculation, on a short deadline. Prep is right the first time.
- Tie forward: the folders and calendar built here are what the rest of the litigation fork audits and litigates from.
