### 5.1.9 Court Hard Rules, SOL Cutoffs & the Master Calendar

> **Lesson** · Day 5 — Settlement, closing & the litigation fork · Module 5: Closing & Settlement (condensed) · scored with ADR & Calendar Compliance Check (`d4m2`)  
> **Topic:** The dates the law sets, and the earlier dates the firm dockets  
> **Continuity:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · Atty. Elijah Thorne · $10,000 BI / $20,000 HOST UM / $10,000 REL UM · 14-Day Gap  
> **Theory extracted from:** `D5#28` Master Timeline & Deadline Calculations · `D4#18` Arbitration Operational Track 1: Critical Timelines & Calendar Hard Rules · Newly authored: weekend roll direction (forward count → next business day; backward count → earlier business day) · Newly authored: SOL internal hard date and 90-day internal filing cutoff (firm rule configured for this training file), kept live after filing until Atty. Thorne releases them · Newly authored: scheduling-order entry (read each verb) and RFAs docketed separately for the deemed-admission risk · Newly authored: calendar-error risk (later or missing costs rights; earlier costs time)

#### 1 · Visual Layout Description

A horizontal master-calendar ribbon across the full slide width on a cream ground, running 10/2026 to 02/2028. Court hard rules stand above the ribbon as navy pins with mono date pills (11/13/2026 · 01/04/2027 · 03/31/2027 · 05/28/2027); forum dates hang below it as orange pins (03/11 · 03/18 · 06/02 · 06/11 · 06/22/2027). At the far right, a red danger flag on 02/06/2028 reads SUNDAY — NEVER RELY ON IT, and two orange pins step back from it: 02/04/2028 INTERNAL HARD DATE and 11/05/2027 FILING CUTOFF. Under the ribbon, two arrow chips pointing opposite ways: a right-pointing FORWARD COUNT → NEXT BUSINESS DAY and a left-pointing BACKWARD COUNT: EARLIER BUSINESS DAY. The argument: the dates the CM dockets sit earlier than the dates the law allows.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Tell Them

*Why:* Keystone's stonewall on the 14-Day Gap moved John Doe's file onto court time. From 09/24/2026 its dates are set by rule, order and statute, and they do not move because a carrier is silent or a client is unreachable. One mis-counted day can concede a fact or end a claim.

1. A hard rule is a date fixed by statute, court rule, court order or a forum's rules. Miss one by a day and the claim can go: a complaint filed after the SOL can be dismissed with prejudice. Every date here is configured for this training file; confirm the court's rules.
2. Count the court's way: exclude the trigger day, include the last. Robert Smith was served 10/14/2026; 30 days → answer due 11/13/2026. Answer periods commonly run 20 to 30 days (21 in federal court).
3. The mailbox trap: many courts add 3 days when a paper is served by mail, and this file's rule set does. E-service is rule-specific: our 12/14/2026 e-served discovery is due back 01/13/2027, nothing added.
4. A forward count that lands on a weekend or court holiday moves to the next business day. A backward count from an event moves to the earlier one. Never roll a backward deadline forward.
5. Unanswered Requests for Admission may be deemed admitted. Docket John Doe's 15 RFAs as their own entry with their own alerts, never folded into “discovery due”.
6. Docket the SOL to internal dates. The firm never relies on a weekend roll-forward: the Sunday SOL, 02/06/2028, becomes an internal hard date of 02/04/2028 (Fri) and an internal filing cutoff 90 days before that (11/06/2027, a Saturday → 11/05/2027).
7. Filing on 09/24/2026 doesn't release the SOL. Whether any claim or party still depends on it is Atty. Thorne's analysis; both internal dates stay live until he releases them in writing.
8. Enter a Scheduling Order the day it arrives and read each verb: mediation “completed by 03/31/2027” is a deadline for the session to be over, not the session date. Plaintiff's expert disclosure (03/15/2027) is ours.
9. The moment a matter is routed to a forum, hard-code its dates on the master calendar and monitor them. Give each an owner, its trigger document, the arithmetic and warning alerts. A deadline that lives only in an email isn't calendared.
10. When a rule is unclear, docket the conservative date and flag it to Atty. Thorne. A date pulled earlier costs time; one pushed later, or left off, costs rights.

> ⚠️ **UPL boundary.** You compute, docket, verify and flag. You may not decide which rule governs, agree an extension with opposing counsel, or tell John Doe what a missed date would do to his case. Those are Atty. Thorne's.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Show Them

*This file's hard rules, counted (as configured for this training file)*

| Trigger | Rule and count | Docket |
|---|---|---|
| Smith served 10/14/2026 | Answer: 30 days | 11/13/2026 (Fri) |
| Discovery mailed 12/01/2026 | 30 + 3 = 01/03/2027 (Sun) → forward | 01/04/2027 (Mon), RFAs too |
| Mediation 03/18/2027 | Statement and binder 7 days before | 03/11/2027 (Thu) |
| Hearing 06/22/2027 | Exhibits 20 days before; brief 10 days before = 06/12 (Sat) → back | 06/02/2027; 06/11/2027 (Fri) |
| SOL 02/06/2028 (Sun) | Hard date before the weekend; cutoff 90 days earlier = 11/06/2027 (Sat) → back | 02/04/2028; 11/05/2027 (Fri) |

**Operational mapping**

| Trigger Event | ➔ Case Manager Task | ➔ Tool/Interface | ➔ Verification Checkpoint |
|---|---|---|---|
| Scheduling Order entered 12/10/2026 | Enter every date with its trigger, rule, owner and alerts; read each verb | Docket System | All six order dates entered that day; “completed by” entered as a deadline |
| Discovery served by mail 12/01/2026 | Count 30 + 3, move the Sunday forward, enter the RFAs separately | Case Docket & Calendar Conflict Resolver | Responses and RFAs both at 01/04/2027; deemed-admitted flag set |
| SOL review | Set the internal hard date and the internal filing cutoff | CMS → Calendar | 02/04/2028 and 11/05/2027 docketed; nothing relies on 02/06/2028 |
| Arbitration invoked 04/05/2027 | Hard-code the hearing, exhibit-exchange and brief dates | CMS → Calendar → Master calendar | 06/02, 06/11 and 06/22/2027 entered with alerts the same day |

**Best practices & pitfalls**

- Record the trigger, the rule and the arithmetic on every entry, so anyone can re-check it.
- Add warning alerts ahead of each hard date.
- Calendar the conservative date when a rule is unclear, and flag it.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: counting the trigger day as day one.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: rolling a backward-counted deadline forward to Monday.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: docketing the SOL on its Sunday and relying on the roll-forward.

#### 3 · Knowledge Check & CM Summary

Verified in ADR & Calendar Compliance Check (taught on Case Flow Day 5) and that day's auto-graded questions.

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Deadline arithmetic | Answer 11/13/2026; responses 01/04/2027 with the 3 mail days; brief 06/11/2027 |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | SOL cutoffs | Internal hard date 02/04/2028 and filing cutoff 11/05/2027, arithmetic shown |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Roll direction | Forward counts move later, backward counts earlier; none rolled the wrong way |
| 🤖 AI | **10%** | Entry completeness | Trigger, rule, owner and alerts on every hard date; RFAs entered separately |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **25%** | Risk judgment | Explains why a late or missing entry is worse than an early one, and dockets the conservative date |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **15%** | Authority boundary | Rule questions and extensions routed to Atty. Thorne; no legal consequence explained to the client |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Open on the SOL: write 02/06/2028 on the board and ask what day of the week it is. Then show the two orange pins stepping back from it.
- Ask the room: “Discovery was mailed to us on 12/01/2026. When are John Doe's responses due?” Make them say every step aloud: trigger day out, 30 days, 3 for mail, Sunday, Monday.
- The most often failed point is direction. Forward counts move to the next business day, backward counts to the earlier one; rolling the 06/12/2027 brief to Monday files it late.
- Every date on this slide is configured for this training file. Say so, and say who confirms the real rule: Atty. Thorne. The CM computes and dockets; the CM never agrees an extension.

### 5.1.10 Mediation vs. Binding Arbitration & the UPL Line at Hearings

> **Lesson** · Day 5 — Settlement, closing & the litigation fork · Module 5: Closing & Settlement (condensed) · scored with ADR & Calendar Compliance Check (`d4m2`)  
> **Topic:** Two neutrals, two standards, one boundary  
> **Continuity:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · Atty. Elijah Thorne · $10,000 BI / $20,000 HOST UM / $10,000 REL UM · 14-Day Gap  
> **Theory extracted from:** `D4#5` Mediation Protocols for CMs: Logistics & Scheduling · `D4#16` Case Phase: Arbitration — The Anatomy of a PI Arbitration · `D4#17` Arbitration: Operational Mindset — From Compromise to Trial-Ready · `D4#19` Arbitration Operational Track 2: Logistics & Setup Protocols · `D4#20` Ethics & Boundaries: What You Can and Cannot Do at Hearings (UPL) · Newly authored: court-ordered mediation compels attendance, not agreement

#### 1 · Visual Layout Description

Two forum cards face each other across the slide, joined by a bold orange arrow labelled COMPROMISE → TRIAL-READY. Left, a navy-soft card: MEDIATION · 03/18/2027 · HON. CARLA BENTON (RET.), with three chips: NON-BINDING · CONFIDENTIAL · FACILITATES. Right, a solid navy card, visibly heavier: BINDING ARBITRATION · 06/22/2027 · LAURA WHITCOMB, ESQ., with three chips: ACTS AS JUDGE · BINDING AWARD · APPEAL LIMITED, and a mono hard-date strip beneath it (06/02 · 06/11 · 06/22/2027). Across the bottom, a full-width red-bordered UPL bar in two halves: CM CAN (observe · log directives · update the CMS) in navy, CM NEVER (argue · speak on the record · speak for John Doe · negotiate) in red. The bar spans both cards: the line is the same at both tables.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Tell Them

*Why:* Two neutrals, three months apart. Hon. Carla Benton (Ret.) can only help the parties agree; Laura Whitcomb, Esq. decides, and her award stands with almost no appeal. Prepare for both alike and you bring a compromise file to a trial-ready hearing.

1. Mediation is facilitated compromise: non-binding, confidential under the governing statute or court rule (exceptions include a signed settlement). Court-ordered means John Doe must attend, not agree. When a deal binds (party signatures, or counsel with authority) varies: confirm the governing rule. Here: 03/18/2027.
2. Binding arbitration, as Harbor Point's clause provides, is an adversarial hearing: the arbitrator acts as judge; courts review her award only on narrow grounds. Confirm the policy and statute; some allow a trial de novo. Here BI settled at mediation, and the UM carriers' dispute over the gap went to a decision.
3. Know the limits. Split limits cap BI per person (Keystone: $10,000, PD separate); a combined single limit is one figure for BI and PD. Damages found are reduced first by the $10,000 BI paid; Harbor Point's add-on UM pays above that up to $20,000, Cardinal State's $10,000 behind it. How an award applies is Atty. Thorne's analysis.
4. Mediation logistics: 3–4 windows from Atty. Thorne, John Doe and opposing counsel before contacting the mediator; a private room (breakouts if virtual); interpreter checked; Atty. Thorne's 2-hour buffer; video open 15 minutes early so he can brief John Doe; you run the technology check only.
5. Every window rejected? Log the pushback that day and escalate to Atty. Thorne. Never accept “a quick video mediation next Tuesday” unchecked.
6. Arbitration setup: align 3–4 blocks with opposing staff; messages to the arbitrator's office (scheduling, fees, retainer) go only as Atty. Thorne directs, in writing, all counsel copied, never on the merits. Route the retainer to accounting, payment confirmed in writing; set the room or secure video.
7. Trial-ready: audit every exhibit, lock every deadline. At the hearing, document testimony and the arbitrator's reactions; log each bench directive as given, calendar it, memo Atty. Thorne that day.
8. The Golden Rule: a CM never argues, speaks on the record or represents John Doe. Asked about a bill: say you are the Case Manager providing administrative assistance, defer to Atty. Thorne, hand him the document.

> ⚠️ **UPL boundary.** At both sessions you run logistics, exhibits, technology and notes. You never speak for John Doe, argue, negotiate or answer a neutral on the merits, or tell John Doe whether to accept a number: that is his call with Atty. Thorne.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Show Them

*The two forums on John Doe's file*

| MEDIATION — compromise · 03/18/2027 | BINDING ARBITRATION — decision · 06/22/2027 |
|---|---|
| Hon. Carla Benton (Ret.) facilitates; she decides nothing. | Laura Whitcomb, Esq. acts as judge; her award binds. |
| Attendance ordered, not agreement; when a deal binds varies. | Court review on narrow grounds only. Trial-ready. |
| Confidential; statement and binder due 03/11/2027. | Exhibits 06/02/2027; brief 06/11/2027. |
| Across: Robert Smith, defended by Keystone. | Across: Harbor Point and Cardinal State. |
| CM: windows, rooms, interpreter, buffer, notes. | CM: retainer, setup, notes, directives. |

**Operational mapping**

| Trigger Event | ➔ Case Manager Task | ➔ Tool/Interface | ➔ Verification Checkpoint |
|---|---|---|---|
| Order requires mediation by 03/31/2027 | Collect 3–4 windows from Atty. Thorne, John Doe and opposing counsel, then contact the mediator's office | Calendaring Simulators | Windows logged before the mediator is contacted; session inside 03/31/2027 |
| Opposing counsel rejects every window | Log the pushback; escalate for an attorney-to-attorney call | CMS → Tasks → Escalate | Logged and escalated the same day |
| Arbitrator's fee schedule and retainer request received | Confirm the schedule; route the retainer to accounting; send internal invites | CMS → Tasks → Accounting · CMS → Calendar | Payment confirmed in writing before the hearing |
| Directive from the bench | Log it as given; calendar it; memo Atty. Thorne | CMS → Case Notes · CMS → Calendar | Directive dated and calendared the same day |

**Best practices & pitfalls**

- Your hearing notes become Atty. Thorne's post-hearing roadmap.
- Collect every party's windows before the neutral's office hears from you.
- Confirm the retainer payment in writing; unpaid retainers delay hearings.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: bringing a mediation-quality binder to an arbitration.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: “helpfully” answering the arbitrator directly.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: logging bench directives the next day from memory.

#### 3 · Knowledge Check & CM Summary

Verified in ADR & Calendar Compliance Check (taught on Case Flow Day 5) and that day's auto-graded questions.

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Forum distinctions | Non-binding, confidential compromise vs. binding award with limited appeal, each tied to its neutral |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Logistics protocol | 3–4 windows first; rooms or breakouts; interpreter; 2-hour buffer; retainer confirmed in writing |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Hearing duties | Observe and document, log directives as given, calendar and memo the same day |
| 🤖 AI | **10%** | Limits in play | Split vs. combined single limit explained; $20,000 and $10,000 UM limits named |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **15%** | Mindset shift | Explains what changes operationally from compromise to trial-ready |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **25%** | UPL boundary | Never speaks for John Doe, argues, negotiates or answers a neutral; the bill script defers to Atty. Thorne |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Open with the two neutrals' names side by side and ask what each can do to John Doe's case. Hon. Carla Benton (Ret.) can't decide it; any deal needs his agreement through Atty. Thorne; Laura Whitcomb, Esq. can decide it.
- Ask the room: “The mediation was court-ordered. Did John Doe have to settle?” Draw out the answer: ordered to attend, never to agree.
- Watch for trainees who treat logistics as clerical. The private room, the 2-hour buffer and the interpreter check are what let Atty. Thorne and John Doe think clearly in a session where nobody else may speak for them.
- Read the Golden Rule aloud, then the bill script. Violating UPL rules puts both the Case Manager and the firm at risk; say it in those words.

### 5.1.11 The Mediation Binder & Exhibit Indexing

> **Lesson** · Day 5 — Settlement, closing & the litigation fork · Module 5: Closing & Settlement (condensed) · scored with Binder Assembly Verification (`d4m3`)  
> **Topic:** Six sections, one master index, and the five-second rule  
> **Continuity:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · Atty. Elijah Thorne · $10,000 BI / $20,000 HOST UM / $10,000 REL UM · 14-Day Gap  
> **Theory extracted from:** `D4#6` Preparing Mediation Binders: Organized Case Summaries · `D4#7` Mediation Binder Section 1: Executive Summary & Administrative Details · `D4#8` Mediation Binder Section 2: The Mediation Briefs · `D4#9` Mediation Binder Section 3: Core Pleadings (The Legal Framework) · `D4#10` Mediation Binder Section 4: Key Evidence & Liability Exhibits · `D4#11` Mediation Binder Section 5: Damages, Financials & Expert Reports · `D4#12` Mediation Binder Section 6: Settlement History & Draft Agreements · `D4#13` Mediation: Quality Assurance & Logistics Auditor · Newly authored: exhibit numbering, Bates ranges and the master index as the one source of truth

#### 1 · Visual Layout Description

An open binder seen from above fills the left two-thirds: six navy divider tabs stepped down its right edge, each with an orange numbered badge ①–⑥ and its section name, and beside each tab a cream card listing this file's documents as mono pills (RPD-26-018842 · EDR · MRI 03/13/2026 · $16,401.40). Across the top runs a slim navy bar, MASTER INDEX — ONE SOURCE OF TRUTH, with column chips Exhibit · Bates · Source · Status and thin orange leader lines running from index rows down into the tabs. Right third: a large stopwatch chip, 5 SECONDS, above a red danger callout: “Drafts and superseded pleadings never enter the binder.” The leader lines run from the index into the binder, never back: that is the argument.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Tell Them

*Why:* Keystone's stonewall on the 14-Day Gap is why John Doe's file is in suit. At the 03/18/2027 mediation Atty. Thorne will have minutes to answer it, so every record, figure and quote must sit in a known place. A binder he must hunt through costs momentum, and can cost a concession.

1. The Mediation Binder is Atty. Thorne's and the mediator's road map: critical records, verified Specials and liability exhibits, indexed and bookmarked so any one is found in five seconds.
2. Six numbered sections in fixed order, each tab matching the master index. Each document sits in one section; others cite its exhibit number.
3. One master index in the CMS is the single source of truth: section, exhibit number, description, date, source, Bates range and status for each document. The binder prints from the index, never the reverse.
4. Exhibit numbers belong to the proceeding (4-1, 4-2…): a late document takes the next number; a set others hold is never renumbered. Bates numbers belong to the page: stamped once at production (DOE 000001).
5. Section 3 holds only the operative pleadings (09/24/2026 complaint, 11/12/2026 answer), no drafts. The answer pleads comparative negligence (out of turn, unbelted), causation (the gap, a “degenerative” MRI) and failure to mitigate; a defense argued but never pleaded is flagged to Atty. Thorne.
6. Section 5's ledger ties line by line to the certified invoices and HR letter, each correction documented (duplicate removed: $6,290.00 → $6,105.00): $14,817.40 medical + $1,584.00 wages = $16,401.40. Future care ($3,400–$6,800) is narrative.
7. Section 6's log fixes the baseline (Keystone's $3,500, “final” 08/12/2026). A draft Limited Release lets Atty. Thorne fix terms in writing before anyone leaves, conditioned on both UM carriers' written consent (each may substitute its own check to keep subrogation). Its scope (BI only, or the pleaded PD claim too) is his call.
8. You are the QA and logistics auditor: sets for Atty. Thorne, the mediator and John Doe (or one digital set), every PDF OCR-searchable, statement delivered by 03/11/2027, binder audited against the index 48 hours out.

> ⚠️ **UPL boundary.** You build the binder and draft the confidential statement for Atty. Thorne's approval. Arguments, settlement positions, every number and each set's contents are his. You never discuss value with the mediator, Atty. Mercer or John Doe.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Show Them

*John Doe's six-section Mediation Binder, and each section's QA rule*

| Section | On this file | QA rule |
|---|---|---|
| 1 · Executive summary & administration | Snapshot sheet; mediation order; 03/18/2027, Hon. Carla Benton (Ret.) | Fee split from the order; contacts current |
| 2 · Briefs | Our confidential statement; the defense's, if exchanged | Ours to the mediator only, by 03/11/2027 |
| 3 · Core pleadings | Complaint 09/24/2026; answer and defenses 11/12/2026 | Operative versions only |
| 4 · Key evidence & liability | RPD-26-018842, Ruth Calloway, doorbell stills, EMS report, EDR, deposition summary | Summary + highlighted transcript pages |
| 5 · Damages, financials & experts | Ledger, Northgate HR letter, MRI, Causation Letter, Gap Explanation Record, PD claim | Ledger = sources: $16,401.40 |
| 6 · Settlement history & drafts | Negotiation Log from 07/20/2026; draft release (Smith, Keystone) | Baseline stated; scope and UM consents flagged for Atty. Thorne |

**Operational mapping**

| Trigger Event | ➔ Case Manager Task | ➔ Tool/Interface | ➔ Verification Checkpoint |
|---|---|---|---|
| Mediation set for 03/18/2027 | Open the master index and seat each document in one section | CMS → Documents → Master index | Every entry has section, exhibit number, Bates range and source |
| Pleadings and evidence indexed | Check each defense pleaded in the answer against Sections 4 and 5 | CMS → Litigation → Pleadings Log | Each pleaded defense has an answering exhibit, or a flag to Atty. Thorne |
| Binder assembled | OCR, bookmark and QA against the index; route for approval | CMS → Documents → Mediation Binder | Text-searchable; approved, then delivered by 03/11/2027 |

**Best practices & pitfalls**

- Start from the 07/20/2026 Demand Package: most of Sections 4 and 5 already exist there.
- Bookmark to the page Atty. Thorne will quote, not to the document's cover.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: handing the attorney an unorganized file the morning of the session.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: a snapshot sheet with stale opposing-counsel contacts.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: sending the confidential statement ten minutes before the session, or to the wrong recipient.

#### 3 · Knowledge Check & CM Summary

Verified in Binder Assembly Verification (taught on Case Flow Day 5) and that day's auto-graded questions.

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Section order and contents | Six sections in order, each with this file's documents |
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Indexing rules | Exhibit numbers per proceeding, Bates stamped once, master index fields, one source of truth |
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Figures and dates | $14,817.40 + $1,584.00 = $16,401.40; pleadings 09/24/2026 and 11/12/2026; delivery 03/11/2027; session 03/18/2027 |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **25%** | Purpose over procedure | Explains how an indexed binder protects Atty. Thorne's position in the room, not just how it is filed |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **15%** | UPL boundary | Arguments, positions and numbers left to Atty. Thorne; the statement kept confidential to the mediator |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Open with a question: “What happens if Atty. Thorne gets an unorganized file the morning of the mediation?” Let the room answer, then say it plainly: he loses momentum, looks unprepared in front of the mediator, and may concede a point because he couldn't find the proof in time.
- Ask the room: “Where do you find the mediator's fee split?” The answer is the mediation order or the parties' stipulation, read, not assumed. Many orders split the fee 50/50; this file's order says what it says.
- Spend real time on the index. Trainees treat the binder as the product; the product is the index, and the binder is printed from it. When two copies disagree, the index decides.
- Section 3 is a legal check run by an operational person: the CM flags an unpled argument, Atty. Thorne decides whether to object. On this file all three defenses were pleaded, so Sections 4 and 5 must answer each one. In Section 5: Dr. Reyes's causation opinion, and any other treating physician's, may itself have to be disclosed by 03/15/2027 under the court's rules. Flag it to Atty. Thorne now (03/04/2027) with the deadline; whether and how each treating physician is disclosed, and whether a retained expert is added, is his call.

### 5.1.12 Arbitration Exhibits & Evidentiary Rules

> **Lesson** · Day 5 — Settlement, closing & the litigation fork · Module 5: Closing & Settlement (condensed) · scored with Binder Assembly Verification (`d4m3`)  
> **Topic:** From a binder that persuades to the record a binding award rests on  
> **Continuity:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · Atty. Elijah Thorne · $10,000 BI / $20,000 HOST UM / $10,000 REL UM · 14-Day Gap  
> **Theory extracted from:** `D4#21` Arbitration Binders: Section 1 — The Arbitration Submissions · `D4#22` Arbitration Binders: Section 2 — Core Litigation Pleadings · `D4#23` Arbitration Binders: Section 3 — Liabilities & Biomechanical Evidence · `D4#24` Arbitration Binders: Section 4 — Proving Bodily Accident Medical Damages · `D4#25` Arbitration Binders: Section 5 — The Prior Medical Shield · `D4#26` Arbitration Binders: Section 6 — Economic Losses & Lien Reconciliations · Newly authored: authentication, business-records certifications, relaxed evidence rules in arbitration, and mediation confidentiality at the hand-off

#### 1 · Visual Layout Description

Two panels joined by a heavy orange arrow labelled THE GAP DISPUTE CARRIES OVER. Left, small and greyed: the Mediation Binder, captioned PERSUADES · NON-BINDING. Right, dominant: six navy tabs ①–⑥ with orange badges, each above a cream card stating its QC rule in one line. Over Tab 1, an orange-outlined THRESHOLD chip: BI EXHAUSTED · $10,000 TENDERED 03/18/2027. Beneath the tabs, an AUTHENTICATION rail of three mono pills: CUSTODIAN CERTIFICATION · NATIVE FILE · CHAIN OF CUSTODY. Bottom-right, a red danger callout: “Not exchanged by 06/02/2027 → may not count.” Beside it, a grey chip: CONFIRM THE FORUM'S RULES. The difference in size between the panels is the argument: this record decides.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Tell Them

*Why:* On 04/05/2027 Harbor Point and Cardinal State took up Keystone's gap argument; Laura Whitcomb, Esq. will decide damages, bindingly. An Arbitration Binder is the record the award rests on: a page missing, illegible or not exchanged by 06/02/2027 may not count.

1. Mediation is non-binding; its binder persuades. A binding award rests on the exhibits, and court review is very limited (some UM clauses allow a trial de novo; confirm the clause and statute). Apply each tab's QC rule as documents are sorted.
2. The threshold leads: UM opens only because BI is exhausted. Prove it with the executed documents (Keystone's $10,000 tender, the UM consents, the Limited Release, Robert Smith's dismissal), never the Mediation Statement.
3. Authenticate every exhibit: show it is what it claims to be. Get each custodian's business-records certification covering every date exhibited: each provider, Riverton Fire (EMS), Riverton PD, Northgate HR. A causation opinion is not a business record. Confirm the forum's rule.
4. Keep video and data native and unaltered, with a chain-of-custody log (doorbell copy secured 02/19/2026). Print stills in color; QC rejects grayscale.
5. Many arbitration rules relax the formal rules of evidence: the arbitrator admits what is relevant and weighs it. Confirm the forum's rules, then build to the strict standard anyway. Admitted is not persuasive.
6. Number claimant exhibits C-1 onward across the tabs, keep the production Bates numbers, and freeze the set once exchanged.
7. Nothing from the confidential mediation crosses over: not our statement, not its offers. Mediation communications are typically confidential and inadmissible; confirm the governing rule.
8. Liens and health-plan payments are generally collateral sources kept from the factfinder; whether paid or billed amounts are the measure, and whether either reaches the arbitrator, varies. The Lien Sheet and the negotiation ledger (offers of compromise) stay in Atty. Thorne's set. Confirm the governing rule.

> ⚠️ **UPL boundary.** You assemble, index, confirm completeness and audit. Atty. Thorne decides what is argued, what is exchanged, what is withheld as privileged and whether an expert is retained. You never argue an exhibit to Laura Whitcomb, Esq. or the carriers.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Show Them

*The UM Arbitration Binder's six tabs, and each tab's QC rule*

| Tab | On John Doe's file | QC rule |
|---|---|---|
| 1 · Submissions | BI exhaustion record; snapshot sheet; final brief; fee disclosures | Retainers paid; final brief |
| 2 · Arbitration pleadings | Demand; Cardinal State's joinder; each carrier's 04/05/2027 position; any Scheduling Order | Operative versions only |
| 3 · Liability & biomechanics | Certified RPD-26-018842; doorbell stills; event data recorder | Identifiers redacted per forum; citation's use: Atty. Thorne; color |
| 4 · Medical damages | Treatment chart; ledger $14,817.40; provider records and bills; MRI 03/13/2026; Dr. Reyes's orders and Causation Letter | Ledger = certified invoices |
| 5 · Prior medical shield | 2021 chiropractic: three low-back visits, resolved; no prior neck complaint; work history | Paired with the MRI read and a medical opinion |
| 6 · Economic losses | Northgate HR letter ($1,584.00) | Wage proof only |
| Attorney's set — not for exchange | Audited Lien Sheet; negotiation ledger; tort complaint and answer | Atty. Thorne decides |

**Operational mapping**

| Trigger Event | ➔ Case Manager Task | ➔ Tool/Interface | ➔ Verification Checkpoint |
|---|---|---|---|
| Arbitration invoked 04/05/2027 | Open the claimant exhibit index and calendar the exchange and the brief | CMS → Litigation → Exhibit index · Docket System | 06/02/2027 exchange and 06/11/2027 brief on the calendar |
| Provider records gathered | Request each custodian's business-records certification | Medical Records Requests | One certification per provider covering every date exhibited |
| Exchange set assembled | Route the set and every flagged item to Atty. Thorne | CMS → Tasks → Escalate | Approved before 06/02/2027; no privileged item in the set |

**Best practices & pitfalls**

- Build to the strict evidence standard even where the forum relaxes it.
- Keep native video and data files untouched; work from copies.
- Request certifications the week the arbitration is invoked; custodians are slow.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: a grayscale print of a crash still.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: a draft brief or a superseded complaint in the exchanged set.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: the Confidential Mediation Statement carried into the Arbitration Binder.

#### 3 · Knowledge Check & CM Summary

Verified in Binder Assembly Verification (taught on Case Flow Day 5) and that day's auto-graded questions.

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Tab order and contents | Six tabs in order with this file's documents; the BI exhaustion record leads |
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | QC rules | Retainer paid, operative arbitration pleadings, redaction, color, ledger = invoices, prior record paired, wage proof only |
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Evidentiary basics | Authentication, business-records certification, chain of custody, “confirm the forum's rules” |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **20%** | Mediation vs. arbitration | Explains why the arbitration record is less forgiving than the Mediation Binder |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **20%** | UPL boundary | Exchange, privilege and argument left to Atty. Thorne; no argument to the arbitrator or the carriers |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Open by holding up the Mediation Binder: “This one persuaded. The next one is evidence.” Then write 06/02/2027 on the board; everything in this lesson counts back from the exchange.
- Ask the room: “Who can say the doorbell video is what we say it is?” Let them reach Margaret Doe as the camera's owner, the 02/19/2026 copy and the chain of custody. Whether anyone testifies is Atty. Thorne's decision.
- Relaxed evidence rules are where trainees get careless. Make them say it back: relaxed admission is not full weight, and the clause may relax nothing at all. Confirm the forum's rules.
- Hold the privilege line. The CM flags; Atty. Thorne decides. An internal memo in an exchanged set cannot be unsent.

### 5.1.13 Identifying Non-Responsive Adjusters, Delayed Records & Scheduling Bottlenecks

> **Lesson** · Day 5 — Settlement, closing & the litigation fork · Module 5: Closing & Settlement (condensed) · scored with Countermeasure Operational Audit (`d4m4`)  
> **Topic:** Name the bottleneck by the date it threatens  
> **Continuity:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · Atty. Elijah Thorne · $10,000 BI / $20,000 HOST UM / $10,000 REL UM · 14-Day Gap  
> **Theory extracted from:** `D4#29` Common Bottlenecks: Mediation (The Compromise Traps) · `D4#30` Common Bottlenecks: Arbitration (The Trial-Ready Landmines) · LSH Claims Specialist: BI liability follow-ups (every 5 business days) and the escalation protocol

#### 1 · Visual Layout Description

A Track B timeline runs across the full slide on a navy rail, hard dates as mono pills: 08/21/2026 · 03/11/2027 · 03/18/2027 · 03/31/2027 · 06/02/2027 · 06/22/2027. Above it, three cream cards with orange numbered badges: ① NON-RESPONSIVE PARTY ② DELAYED RECORD ③ SCHEDULING, each dropping an orange leader line to the date it threatens. Card ① is the largest, with a red danger callout beneath it: “$10,000 carrier · $40,000 frozen.” Right edge, behind a thin vertical rule, two stacked chips: MEDIATION — a gap costs leverage (navy) and ARBITRATION — a gap costs evidence (red). The argument: a bottleneck is measured by the hard date it threatens, not by how irritating it is.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Tell Them

*Why:* On Track B, Keystone held at $3,500 on the 14-Day Gap, then went silent: a $10,000 carrier froze the whole $40,000 stack and forked the file into suit. Bottlenecks look like a quiet inbox, a record “on its way”, an unconfirmed date. Anticipate them, and clear them before they cost a deadline or an exhibit.

1. A bottleneck is any dependency that can stop the file reaching a hard date. Log it the day it starts, with the hard date it threatens; age it daily.
2. Non-responsive adjuster: follow up every 5 business days and log date, time, method and outcome. Keystone left 08/24 and 08/31/2026 unanswered, then the 09/02 escalation.
3. Silence is a position. Record it as dated facts (“no response to 08/24, 08/31 or 09/02”), never as “adjuster busy”. With BI unexhausted, Host UM and Relative UM cannot open.
4. Delayed records: a request unanswered after 14 days is a bottleneck, not a wait. The biggest mediation bottleneck is an unverified ledger: Specials $16,401.40, Riverton Spine & Rehab audited to $6,105.00, Peach State asserting $1,860.00.
5. Missing decision-maker: a representative without authority to pay sinks a mediation. Verified ledgers reach Keystone via defense counsel, as Atty. Thorne directs, in time for an authority raise.
6. Brief alignment: the confidential statement and binder reach Hon. Carla Benton (Ret.) by 03/11/2027 with every critical exhibit (MRI, Causation Letter, Police Report, doorbell video). A late brief stalls her.
7. Arbitration is binding: no second hearing. An exhibit not exchanged by 06/02/2027 can be excluded; one that is illegible or not a fair and accurate copy can be excluded or given little weight. Print stills in color from the native file so nothing turns on quality.
8. Shield the prior: the 2021 low-back chiropractic visits will be used to discount the neck injury. Package them with an expert declaration, as Atty. Thorne directs.
9. Scheduling: sync the neutral's, the parties' and Atty. Thorne's windows before a date is offered. Mediation is completed by 03/31/2027; the arbitration brief is docketed 06/11/2027. A date outside the order is no date.

> ⚠️ **UPL boundary.** You may chase, document, flag and report a bottleneck. You may not choose a legal remedy (a subpoena, a motion, an extension), discuss value with a carrier, or tell John Doe what Keystone's silence means for his claim. Those are Atty. Thorne's.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Show Them

*Five bottlenecks on the Track B file, their early signals and the dates they threaten*

| Bottleneck | On this file | Early signal | Hard date threatened |
|---|---|---|---|
| Non-responsive adjuster | Keystone (Dana Whitfield) after its “final” $3,500 | Second follow-up unanswered | UM can't open; SOL cutoff 11/05/2027 |
| Delayed record | Riverton Spine & Rehab's Final Ledger | No answer 14 days after the request | Binder to the mediator 03/11/2027 |
| Missing decision-maker | Keystone's mediation attendee | Authority unconfirmed 10 days out | Mediation 03/18/2027 |
| Scheduling | A proposed mediation date | Falls after the order's deadline | Mediation completed by 03/31/2027 |
| Evidentiary exclusion | Arbitration exhibits, prior-medical shield | An exhibit incomplete 10 days out | Exhibit exchange 06/02/2027 |

**Operational mapping**

| Trigger Event | ➔ Case Manager Task | ➔ Tool/Interface | ➔ Verification Checkpoint |
|---|---|---|---|
| A follow-up goes unanswered | Log it and date the next follow-up on the 5-business-day cadence | CMS → Communications Log | Date, time, method and outcome logged; next follow-up dated |
| The second follow-up goes unanswered | Open a bottleneck entry: blocker, start date, hard date threatened | CMS → Case File → Bottleneck Log | Start date is the first unanswered request (Keystone: 08/24/2026) |
| A proposed ADR date arrives | Check it against every party's window and the hard-date calendar before anyone accepts it | Case Docket & Calendar Conflict Resolver | No mediation date after 03/31/2027 reaches Atty. Thorne as available |
| A binder or exhibit dependency is open 10 days out | Red-flag it and tell Atty. Thorne | CMS → Case File → Risk Flags | Flag dated 03/01/2027 for the 03/11/2027 binder |

**Best practices & pitfalls**

- Anticipate and eliminate: a bottleneck found at the deadline was found too late.
- The single biggest mediation bottleneck is an unverified medical ledger.
- Follow up in writing as well as by phone; a call alone leaves nothing to prove the silence with.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: a confidential statement that reaches the mediator ten minutes before the session.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: damage photos or video stills printed in grayscale for a binding hearing.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: logging silence as “adjuster busy” instead of the dates that prove it.

#### 3 · Knowledge Check & CM Summary

Verified in Countermeasure Operational Audit (taught on Case Flow Day 5) and that day's auto-graded questions.

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Bottleneck classification | Each problem placed in its family (party, record, scheduling) with this file's example |
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Dates and hard dates | 08/24, 08/31, 09/02, 09/04/2026 and 03/11, 03/18, 03/31, 06/02, 06/11/2027 matched to the right bottleneck |
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Mediation and arbitration traps | Unverified ledger, missing decision-maker, late brief; exclusion, unshielded prior, missed cutoff |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **25%** | Anticipation | Names each bottleneck from its early signal, before the hard date makes it obvious |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **15%** | UPL boundary | Chases and reports; never chooses a legal remedy, discusses value or explains Keystone's silence to John Doe |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Open on the frozen stack: put $10,000 beside $40,000 and ask, “How much of John Doe's coverage did Keystone's silence lock?” All of it. That is what one unmanaged bottleneck costs.
- Ask the room: “When did the Keystone bottleneck start: 08/21, 08/24 or 09/04?” The lapse on 08/21 was a negotiating outcome; the silence began with the first unanswered request, 08/24. The log has to show it from that day, not from the day it hurt.
- Watch for trainees treating mediation and arbitration bottlenecks alike. A thin Mediation Binder costs leverage; a missing arbitration exhibit can be excluded from a binding hearing. Evidentiary exclusion is the hardest landmine to recover from.
- The decision-maker trap: an adjuster who arrives with authority well below the limits means the package and verified ledgers reached the carrier too late for an authority raise. Keystone tendered the $10,000 limits at mediation on 03/18/2027; someone in the room had to be able to pay them.

### 5.1.14 Escalation Workflows & Supervisory Reporting Guidelines

> **Lesson** · Day 5 — Settlement, closing & the litigation fork · Module 5: Closing & Settlement (condensed) · scored with Countermeasure Operational Audit (`d4m4`)  
> **Topic:** The ladder decides who hears; the report decides whether they can act  
> **Continuity:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · Atty. Elijah Thorne · $10,000 BI / $20,000 HOST UM / $10,000 REL UM · 14-Day Gap  
> **Theory extracted from:** `D1#45` Common Bottlenecks: Escalation Protocol & the Escalation Ladder · `D4#31` Case Manager Operational Countermeasures: The 48-Hour Mandate · `D5#32` Advanced KPIs for Litigation Case Managers · LSH Claims Specialist: escalation protocol (supervisor review, internal flag, certified request for a written position)

#### 1 · Visual Layout Description

Left third: a three-rung ladder drawn bottom-up, navy rungs with orange numbered badges — ① SOURCE ② SUPERVISOR · NOTICE OF DELAY ③ ATTY. THORNE — a 48 H mono pill on the rail between each pair. Under the ladder, a red danger strip: “Don't skip. Don't sit.” Right two-thirds: the bottleneck report as a white memo card with a navy header bar, five fields called out by orange badges — ① Blocker ② Started ③ Threatens ④ Tried ⑤ The ask — each filled in with Keystone's dates in mono type. The ask is boxed in orange and is the only field in bold. The argument: escalation is a sequence, and the report is what lets counsel act in one read.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Tell Them

*Why:* An escalation without the dates, the threatened deadline and a clear ask makes Atty. Thorne do your research before he can decide. The ladder decides who hears about a bottleneck and when; the report decides whether they can act on it in one read.

1. Climb one rung at a time and log each attempt. Rung 1: the source, on the follow-up cadence. Rung 2: its supervisor or office manager, with a written Notice of Delay. Rung 3: Atty. Thorne.
2. The Notice of Delay states facts: what is outstanding, since when, the hard date it threatens, and a request to review the delay. It never argues bad faith or the insured's exposure; that analysis is Atty. Thorne's.
3. The 48-Hour Mandate times the climb: after the second unanswered follow-up, each step gets 48 hours before silence moves the file up. Keystone: second follow-up 08/31 → supervisor 09/02 → silence 09/04 → Atty. Thorne; impasse memo 09/08/2026.
4. It also audits: 48 hours before every hard date, check ledgers against EOBs, the binder for completeness, the set for OCR. What it finds is escalated that day.
5. Rung 3 is where legal tools live: a certified request for a written position, a subpoena, a motion to compel, an extension. You may name it in the ask as a question (“Do you want a records subpoena?”); Atty. Thorne decides, signs and sends.
6. Don't skip, don't sit. Skipping Rungs 1 and 2 spends his time on a supervisor's problem. With a hard date 10 days out, red-flag and tell him in parallel while the rungs continue.
7. Every report carries five fields: the blocker; the date it started; the hard date it threatens; what was tried (each rung, date, method, result); the ask: the decision needed and by when. One ask per blocker, nearest hard date first.
8. Zero defaults: by the hard date, the filing is ready with what you have, marked “Pending Final Verification”, or Atty. Thorne holds the extension in the form the rule requires (a stipulation where court rules allow, a court order, or the arbitrator's written order). Until it is docketed, the date stands.
9. Report up, not out: Atty. Thorne gets the Bottleneck Log weekly, each open item with its age and days to its hard date. A party with counsel is reached only through counsel, as he directs.

> ⚠️ **UPL boundary.** You may escalate, report and ask Atty. Thorne whether to use a legal tool. You may not choose it, sign or send it, contact a represented party, or tell John Doe what a delay means for his claim; you tell him what the firm is doing and route the rest to Atty. Thorne.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Show Them

*The five-field report, filled in for Keystone as it reached Atty. Thorne on 09/08/2026*

| Field | Keystone Mutual · KM-26-440871 | Why it is there |
|---|---|---|
| Blocker | Dana Whitfield holds at $3,500 (“final”, 08/12) and has stopped responding | Names who owes what |
| Started | 08/24/2026, the first unanswered follow-up | Ages the problem honestly |
| Threatens | BI exhaustion: the $20,000 Host UM and $10,000 Relative UM stay closed; SOL filing cutoff 11/05/2027 | Sets the urgency by date, not tone |
| Tried | Follow-ups 08/24 and 08/31; supervisor escalation 09/02; no response 09/04 | Proves the rungs were climbed |
| The ask | Your decision on how the firm proceeds, needed this week; full record attached. No recommendation on filing. | One decision, ready to make |

**Operational mapping**

| Trigger Event | ➔ Case Manager Task | ➔ Tool/Interface | ➔ Verification Checkpoint |
|---|---|---|---|
| Rung 1 unanswered on its cadence | Send a written Notice of Delay to the supervisor or office manager | Email Workspace · CMS → Communications Log | Notice names the item, start date and hard date; date, time and method logged |
| Rung 2 unanswered for 48 hours | Escalate to Atty. Thorne with the five-field report | CMS → Tasks → Escalate | All five fields complete; one ask per blocker |
| Weekly reporting day | Send the Bottleneck Log with each item's age and days to its hard date | CMS → Case File → Bottleneck Log | No open item without an owner, an age and a hard date |
| A hard date arrives with the blocker open | Prepare the filing or exchange with what you have, marked “Pending Final Verification”, for Atty. Thorne's sign-off, or confirm his extension, in the form the rule requires, is docketed | CMS → Calendar · CMS → Documents → Outgoing | Zero defaults: every hard date met or covered by an extension in the form the rule requires |

**Best practices & pitfalls**

- Climb one rung at a time and document each attempt.
- Lead the report with the nearest hard date.
- Write the ask as one decision and the date it is needed by.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: going straight to Atty. Thorne before the source and its supervisor have been tried.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: “Keystone still isn't responding, please advise”, with no dates.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: letting a deadline pass because a record was late.

#### 3 · Knowledge Check & CM Summary

Verified in Countermeasure Operational Audit (taught on Case Flow Day 5) and that day's auto-graded questions.

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Ladder sequence | Source → supervisor with a Notice of Delay → Atty. Thorne, 48 hours per rung, each attempt logged |
| 🤖 AI | **25%** | Report fields | All five fields, with Keystone's dates 08/24, 08/31, 09/02 and 09/04/2026 correct |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Mandate and zero defaults | 48-hour audit before every hard date; every hard date met with what is in hand, marked pending, or covered by an extension in the form the rule requires |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **20%** | Report clarity | Atty. Thorne can act in one read; facts, not adjectives |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **20%** | UPL boundary | Asks, as a question, without choosing a legal tool; no contact with a represented party; nothing on value to John Doe |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Open with a bad escalation on screen: “Keystone still not responding, please advise.” Ask what Atty. Thorne has to look up before he can act. Every answer is a field the report should have carried.
- Ask: “Who should have heard about the Keystone silence first: Dana Whitfield's supervisor or Atty. Thorne?” The supervisor, on 09/02. Skipping the rung spends counsel's time on a problem a claims supervisor might have solved.
- Watch the zero-default rule. Under pressure, trainees hold an exchange to wait for one record. The rule is: send what you have, marked pending, or have Atty. Thorne's extension in the form the rule requires (stipulation, court order or arbitrator's order), docketed. Never neither.
- Legal nuance: a certified request for a written position, a subpoena and a motion to compel are counsel's tools, and the rules for each (who may issue it, notice, time to comply) vary by court and forum. The Case Manager may name them in the ask; Atty. Thorne decides, signs and sends.

### 5.1.15 Litigation Tracking: the Pleadings Log & the Discovery Calendar

> **Lesson** · Day 5 — Settlement, closing & the litigation fork · Module 5: Closing & Settlement (condensed) · scored with Litigation Support Assessment (`d5m1`)  
> **Topic:** Managing litigation tracking systems, pleadings logs & discovery calendars  
> **Continuity:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · Atty. Elijah Thorne · $10,000 BI / $20,000 HOST UM / $10,000 REL UM · 14-Day Gap  
> **Theory extracted from:** `D5#1` Welcome to Day 5: Stepping into Litigation · `D5#2` The Case Manager's Tactical Role in Litigation: The Litigation Lifecycle · `D5#3` Phase 1: The Pleadings & Service Stage · `D5#4` Phase 2: Discovery — A. Interrogatories & Requests for Production · `D5#21` The Litigation Toolkit: The Litigation Document Index · `D5#24` The 3 Golden Rules of Litigation File Maintenance · `D5#26` Advanced Workflow Deep-Dive: The Complaint / Petition · `D5#27` Written Discovery: Interrogatories, RFPs & RFAs · `D5#28` Master Timeline & Deadline Calculations · `D5#31` Litigation File Architecture · Newly authored: whether e-service adds days (none on this file's rule set; the federal example)

#### 1 · Visual Layout Description

A full-width horizontal court timeline on a navy rail runs from 09/24/2026 to 01/13/2027, with orange numbered badges on each trigger: ① Complaint filed ② Smith served 10/14 ③ Answer 11/12 ④ Discovery mailed 12/01 ⑤ Scheduling Order 12/10 ⑥ Our set e-served 12/14. From each trigger a thin orange arrow drops to its due date in a mono pill below the rail. The 12/01 arrow is drawn in three visible segments, + 30 · + 3 MAIL · SUN → MON, so the count reads physically. Right edge, a red danger callout on a cream card: “15 RFAs · unanswered = deemed admitted.” Bottom strip: the five numbered litigation sub-folders as mono pills. The argument: every date on the calendar is a calculation anyone can re-check.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Tell Them

*Why:* On 09/24/2026 John Doe's file became Civil Action No. 26-CV-04417. An adjuster's deadline bends; a court's does not. The Pleadings Log and Discovery Calendar prove, every day, that nothing filed, served or owed has slipped.

1. Litigation is not a longer negotiation. Our demand lapsed 08/21/2026; from the 09/24/2026 filing on, the court's rules set the pace.
2. Three phases: pleadings (filing, service, Answer); discovery (calendar, gather, meet the client; most of your time); trial prep (records, subpoenas, binders).
3. The Complaint has five parts: caption, jurisdiction, facts, causes of action, prayer for relief. Atty. Thorne drafts it; before signature you verify the filing date against the SOL and the defendant's exact legal name (a business: its registered agent).
4. Service proves the defendant knew. Log every attempt; unserved at day 14 (10/08/2026, third failed attempt), escalate to Atty. Thorne for Skip-Trace authority, run it on his approval (10/09/2026) and update him; alternative service is his call. Smith was served 10/14/2026; affidavit filed 10/16/2026.
5. Know the five foundational documents: the Complaint (SOL proof), the Answer (the roadmap), Interrogatories (the deadline clock), RFPs (you compile; Atty. Thorne reviews and redacts), RFAs (the danger zone).
6. Read the Answer as a roadmap: comparative negligence, causation (the 14-Day Gap, a “degenerative” MRI), failure to mitigate. Each defense is evidence to pull.
7. An RFA not answered in time may be deemed admitted. The 15 RFAs of 12/01/2026 get their own row and a red flag, and Atty. Thorne hears the hour they land.
8. Count court time by the hard rules, as docketed in the calendar audit (12/01/2026 by mail → 01/04/2027). The log does not re-derive the date; it carries the arithmetic in the row and the proof of compliance beside it. Unsure? Calendar the conservative date and confirm the rule.
9. Discovery received: calendar it that day, pull what pre-lit holds, book a 60-minute Discovery Intake Meeting; never mail John Doe 70 requests (25 + 30 + 15). He signs the verification under oath.
10. DOE, JOHN - CASE FILE (built 09/10/2026) holds 01_PLEADINGS · 02_SERVICE_DOCS · 03_DISCOVERY_PLAINTIFF_TO_DEFENDANT · 04_DISCOVERY_DEFENDANT_TO_PLAINTIFF · 05_SUBPOENAS, apart from pre-lit mail. Name PDFs Document_Status.pdf (Complaint_Filed_Stamped.pdf), never scan001.pdf.

> ⚠️ **UPL boundary.** You track, calendar, compile and draft for review. Atty. Thorne decides what is admitted, denied or objected to and signs every filing; you never tell John Doe how to answer.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Show Them

*The master timeline: every deadline is a trigger plus a count you can re-check*

| Trigger | Next action | Standard deadline | On this file |
|---|---|---|---|
| Complaint filed | Serve defendant | Often 90–120 days, by rule | Filed 09/24/2026; served 10/14/2026 |
| Defendant served | Answer | 20–30 days (federal: 21) | 10/14/2026 + 30 = 11/13/2026 |
| Discovery mailed | Responses | 30 days + mail days by rule (3 on this file's rule set; some courts add 5) | 12/01/2026 + 33 = 01/03/2027, Sunday → 01/04/2027 |
| Discovery e-served by consent | Responses | 30 days; added days vary by court | 12/14/2026 + 30 = 01/13/2027 |
| Motion filed | Response | 14–21 days, by local rule | Calendar on receipt |

**Operational mapping**

| Trigger Event | ➔ Case Manager Task | ➔ Tool/Interface | ➔ Verification Checkpoint |
|---|---|---|---|
| Atty. Thorne authorizes suit | Verify the filing date against the SOL and Robert Smith's exact legal name and address before signature | CMS → Litigation → Pleadings Log | Filed 09/24/2026, inside the 02/04/2028 internal hard date; Robert Smith's legal name confirmed from an independent record (for example, the Declaration Page or property or DMV records), not the Police Report alone |
| Complaint filed | File the stamped complaint in 01_PLEADINGS and log the filing | CMS → Documents | Filed the same day; no pre-lit correspondence in the sub-folders |
| A service attempt fails | Log it; still unserved at day 14 (here 10/08/2026, the third failed attempt), escalate to Atty. Thorne for Skip-Trace authority with the attempt log attached, run it on his approval (result 10/09/2026), and send him the status update; alternative service is his call | CMS → Tasks → Escalate | Each attempt logged with date and address; status update dated |
| Discovery or RFAs land | Calendar the response date with its arithmetic; alert Atty. Thorne the hour RFAs arrive | CMS → Calendar · Docket System | Due date = trigger + 30 (+ 3 for mail), weekend rolled; RFA alert time-stamped |

**Best practices & pitfalls**

- Write the trigger, the method of service and the arithmetic into every calendar entry.
- Every interrogatory set we send asks the three baseline questions: all insurance (primary, excess, umbrella), course and scope of employment, and surveillance. The file's verified stack is $40,000 (BI → Host UM → Relative UM); what any answer means for coverage is a question for Atty. Thorne.
- Property Damage is pleaded too (the Civic's Loss of Use and Diminished Value): physical evidence of the impact, and something the defense will test.
- In the Discovery Intake Meeting, walk John Doe through every request and record his own answers, in his words, into the response template for Atty. Thorne's review; flag every request that may call for an objection or privilege (including the scope of the 2021 chiropractic notes) for his decision; never suggest an answer. Then compile what the RFPs ask for: Northgate's wage letter, the Medical Records and bills, the 2021 chiropractic notes.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: a perfect Complaint filed one day after the SOL.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: missing a court deadline the way you might let an adjuster's deadline slide.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: an executed affidavit of service left sitting in email.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: counting the trigger day as day one.

#### 3 · Knowledge Check & CM Summary

Verified in Litigation Support Assessment (taught on Case Flow Day 5) and that day's auto-graded questions.

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Time computation | 11/13/2026, 01/04/2027 and 01/13/2027 derived with the trigger day excluded, mail days applied only to mail, the weekend rolled |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Document index | Complaint, Answer, Interrogatories, RFPs and RFAs each named with its role on this file |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Answer roadmap | Comparative negligence, causation and failure to mitigate each tied to evidence on file |
| 🤖 AI | **10%** | File architecture | DOE, JOHN - CASE FILE and the five numbered sub-folders named exactly |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **15%** | Litigation mindset | Treats court deadlines as absolute; explains why a lawsuit is not a longer negotiation |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **10%** | Client handling | Discovery Intake Meeting, not a mailed questionnaire; the verification page explained without coaching |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **15%** | UPL boundary | No admit-or-deny call and no advice on answers; strategy and signatures left to Atty. Thorne |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Open with the question the old course asked, made specific: “Our 07/20/2026 Time-Limited Demand to Keystone lapsed unaccepted on 08/21/2026, and the 48-Hour Mandate got no answer. What is our very next legal step, and who decides it?” Suit, and Atty. Thorne decides. Then name the day: litigation support now, then the Property Damage dispute, deposition preparation, and liability disputes and MIA clients.
- Ask the room: “Which phase will take most of your time, and why?” Discovery. Seventy requests landed on 12/01/2026, and every one of them has a clock.
- Work the 12/01/2026 count on the board, one step at a time. Watch for trainees who count 12/01 as day one, stop at 12/31, or forget that 01/03/2027 is a Sunday.
- Legal nuance: the 30-day periods, the 3 mail days and the weekend roll-forward are configured for this training file. Whether e-service adds days differs by court (the federal rules stopped adding them for electronic service in 2016). Teach the method and the habit of confirming the rule, not a number to memorize.

### 5.1.16 Trial Preparation Checklists & Litigation KPIs

> **Lesson** · Day 5 — Settlement, closing & the litigation fork · Module 5: Closing & Settlement (condensed) · scored with Litigation Support Assessment (`d5m1`)  
> **Topic:** Trial preparation checklists & tracking litigation KPIs  
> **Continuity:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · Atty. Elijah Thorne · $10,000 BI / $20,000 HOST UM / $10,000 REL UM · 14-Day Gap  
> **Theory extracted from:** `D5#2` The Case Manager's Tactical Role in Litigation: The Litigation Lifecycle · `D5#5` Phase 2: Discovery — B. Deposition Coordination & Preparation · `D5#6` Phase 3: The Trial & Mediation Preparation Stage · `D5#7` The Litigation Mindset: Critical Traps & KPIs · `D5#24` The 3 Golden Rules of Litigation File Maintenance · `D5#25` Key Performance Indicators (KPIs) for Document Management · `D5#32` Advanced KPIs for Litigation Case Managers · LSH Claims Specialist: liability acceptance post-lawsuit (the defense pivots to causation and damages) · Newly authored: a scheduling-order date moves only by court order

#### 1 · Visual Layout Description

Upper half: a horizontal trial-prep runway on a navy line from 01/20/2027 to 09/13/2027 with seven orange-numbered milestones: ① Deposition 02/18 ② Our experts 03/15 ③ Mediation by 03/31 ④ Defense experts 04/15 ⑤ Discovery closes 05/28 ⑥ Pretrial 08/12 ⑦ Trial calendar 09/13. Before each milestone, three small tick marks labelled 30 · 14 · 7. A red danger band drops from ⑤: nothing that needs an answer is scheduled past it. Lower half: three cream cards in a row, the KPI families, each with mono pills for its targets (48 h · 14 days · 1 hour · 7 days · 30 days · 10 days). The argument: the order sets the dates; the KPIs prove you are ahead of them.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Tell Them

*Why:* The Scheduling Order entered 12/10/2026 now runs John Doe's case to a 09/13/2027 trial calendar, and every date in it is absolute. A trial-prep checklist turns those dates into owned tasks; the KPIs prove, week by week, that each task is ahead of its deadline rather than behind it.

1. The Scheduling Order controls: pin it to the front of the file, 30-, 14- and 7-day alerts on every date. Our experts 03/15/2027, theirs 04/15/2027, mediation by 03/31/2027, discovery closes 05/28/2027, pretrial 08/12/2027, trial calendar 09/13/2027.
2. Every extension is in writing: once Atty. Thorne agrees, draft the same-day written confirmation for his signature (“Per our phone call, we agree to extend … to [date]”); it goes out in his name, never the CM's. A scheduling-order date usually moves only by court order.
3. Trap 1: treating court deadlines like adjuster deadlines. A missed disclosure can keep a causation opinion out of trial.
4. Trap 2: letting the client talk to the other side. Once the firm represents John Doe, and above all in suit, everything from the other side goes through counsel: if Keystone or a defense investigator reaches John Doe, step in, end it and tell Atty. Thorne that day.
5. For John Doe's deposition (02/18/2027, 10:00 a.m., videoconference): confirm Riverton Court Reporting, send the link, run a technology check, align three calendars, and send the attorney-approved prep packet a week out, by 02/11/2027.
6. Prepare the process, never the testimony. The packet carries the ground rules (listen, pause, never guess, tell the truth); what John Doe says is for Atty. Thorne's prep sessions.
7. Once suit forces the liability proof, defenses often pivot to causation and damages; here the 14-Day Gap is the trap. Assemble the Gap Record Atty. Thorne preps from: EMS report, Attendance Record, texts, receipt, MRI read, Causation Letter.
8. Run final records from every provider before expert disclosure and again before pretrial; never copy the pre-lit package.
9. Draft trial subpoenas for the witnesses Atty. Thorne names; build indexed, tabbed binders from the litigation file.
10. Write every KPI as a formula with a target, read it from the log, and audit the docket weekly. A miss is recorded and routed, never smoothed over.

> ⚠️ **UPL boundary.** You schedule, assemble, track and relay. You never agree an extension, choose an expert or witness, discuss the merits with Atty. Mercer's office (logistics only, as Atty. Thorne directs), or coach what John Doe says at his deposition.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Show Them

*Nine litigation KPIs in three families, each with a target you can measure*

1. **Pleadings & service** — Service Check: a server assigned within 48 hours of filing. 14-Day Service Milestone: unserved at day 14 (10/08/2026) → escalate to Atty. Thorne for Skip-Trace authority, run it on his approval, status update to him.
2. **Discovery & documents** — 48-Hour Docket Rule. RFA Priority Check: Atty. Thorne alerted within the hour. Discovery Countdown: drafts to him 7+ days before the deadline. Clean Discovery Check: no blanks, verification page attached. Zero-Default: no deadline passes without a response or a signed written extension.
3. **Client & trial** — 30-Day Client Pulse: every litigation client called at least every 30 days, news or not. Subpoena Audit: every outstanding subpoena reviewed every 10 days.

**Operational mapping**

| Trigger Event | ➔ Case Manager Task | ➔ Tool/Interface | ➔ Verification Checkpoint |
|---|---|---|---|
| Scheduling Order entered 12/10/2026 | Pin it to the front of the file; calendar all six dates with three alerts each | CMS → Calendar · Docket System | Six dates exact; 18 alerts set; order filed in 01_PLEADINGS |
| An extension is agreed by phone | Confirm Atty. Thorne's approval, draft the written confirmation for his signature, recalendar | Email Workspace · CMS → Calendar | Confirmation sent the same day over Atty. Thorne's name; no scheduling-order date moved without a court order |
| Deposition noticed | Book the reporter, the link and the technology check; send the attorney-approved packet a week out | CMS → Calendar · Communications Log | Packet sent by 02/11/2027; receipt confirmed with John Doe and logged |
| Weekly docket audit | Take every KPI reading from the log and route each miss | CMS → Litigation → KPI tracker · Tasks → Escalate | Each KPI shows its formula, target and a dated reading |

**Best practices & pitfalls**

- Every scheduling-order date gets three alerts: 30, 14 and 7 days out.
- Call every litigation client at least every 30 days, even when nothing has happened in court.
- Put the subpoena audit on a recurring 10-day reminder.
- Trial subpoenas go only to the witnesses Atty. Thorne names; the candidates on this file are Ruth Calloway, the investigating Riverton PD officer, Dr. Reyes and the other treating physicians.
- Reset John Doe's expectations early: a deposition is a long, recorded conversation on a screen, not a courtroom scene.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: a phone-only extension.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: trial binders built from the pre-lit records.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: sending the deposition prep packet the day before.

#### 3 · Knowledge Check & CM Summary

Verified in Litigation Support Assessment (taught on Case Flow Day 5) and that day's auto-graded questions.

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Scheduling-order dates | All six dates exact, each with 30-, 14- and 7-day alerts |
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | KPI definitions | Each KPI named with its target: 48 hours, 14 days, 1 hour, 7 days, zero defaults, 30 days, 10 days |
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Trial-prep content | Final records run, subpoenas, binders and deposition logistics each tied to an order date |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **15%** | Litigation mindset | Treats court dates as absolute; explains why an extension is written and authorized |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **10%** | Deposition boundary | Prepares documents and logistics, never testimony |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **15%** | UPL boundary | No extension agreed, no expert or witness chosen, no merits discussed with the defense; contact from the other side routed to Atty. Thorne |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Open by holding up the Scheduling Order: “This one page now outranks every adjuster letter in the file. Why?” Because a judge signed it, and every date in it is absolute.
- Ask the room: “Defense counsel will spend John Doe's deposition on the 14-Day Gap. What is your job before 02/18/2027, and where does it stop?” Documents, logistics and the packet; it stops at what John Doe says.
- Walk the three KPI families and insist on numbers. A KPI without a target is a wish, and “stay on top of RFAs” is not a KPI.
- Legal nuance: whether a late disclosure excludes evidence, and whether counsel can agree an extension without the court, depends on the court and the order. Teach the habit, not the answer: in writing, through Atty. Thorne, rule confirmed.

### 5.1.17 Repair Estimates vs. ACV & Loss-of-Use Allocations

> **Lesson** · Day 5 — Settlement, closing & the litigation fork · Module 5: Closing & Settlement (condensed) · scored with PD Dispute Evaluation (`d5m2`)  
> **Topic:** Repairable or total, and what each day without the Civic is worth  
> **Continuity:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · Atty. Elijah Thorne · $10,000 BI / $20,000 HOST UM / $10,000 REL UM · 14-Day Gap  
> **Theory extracted from:** `D5#34` Property Damage in Litigation: Types of Damages You Can Recover · `D5#35` Property Damage: Calculating the Value of the Claim · `D2#6` Master Audit Checklist: Property Damage (PD) Deep Dive · LSH Claims Specialist: Property Damage and collision coverage, Loss of Use, tow and storage · Newly authored: a carrier's rental cap against the reasonable-repair-time measure of Loss of Use (Restatement (Second) of Torts § 928(b) as an example)

#### 1 · Visual Layout Description

A horizontal threshold gauge across the top half, the same instrument as Day 1's, now carrying John Doe's numbers: a bar from $0 to ACV $17,600.00, a bold navy THRESHOLD marker at 75% labelled $13,200.00, a grey chip at $6,940.00 (estimate, 39.4%) and an orange chip at $9,255.00 (final, 52.6%), joined by an orange arrow tagged +$2,315.00 A-PILLAR SUPPLEMENT. Lower half: a 78-day calendar ribbon from 02/06 to 04/24/2026 in three segments: red 12 days, navy 30 days (RENTAL PAID $1,260.00), red 36 days. A brace under the two red segments reads LOSS OF USE 48 × $42.00 = $2,016.00. The argument: Keystone paid for the middle and nothing on either side.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Tell Them

*Why:* On Track B the Civic is no longer a side claim: its Loss of Use and Diminished Value are pleaded in the suit, and every figure will be tested in discovery. The repair-against-ACV arithmetic decides which measure of damages applies, and the loss-of-use calendar is the transportation record that explains the 14-Day Gap.

1. When negligence damages property, the law aims to make the owner whole: John Doe's position before 02/06/2026, never a windfall. Test every PD figure against it.
2. Three damage types are recoverable. Economic (special): repair, tow and storage, rental, Loss of Use, Diminished Value. Non-economic (general): pain and suffering, on the injury side. Punitive: to punish egregious conduct such as DUI; none is pleaded here.
3. PD is also evidence of impact severity: the $2,315.00 teardown supplement (03/02/2026) is structural, the A-pillar, showing hidden damage for the PD claim and force for the injury claim.
4. Courts generally award the lesser of the repair cost or the drop in market value: a car costing more to fix than it was worth is a Total Loss, capped at its value. Confirm Keystone's threshold basis in writing.
5. Run it on the final bill: $6,940.00 + $2,315.00 = $9,255.00, 52.6% of the $17,600.00 ACV, under Keystone's 75% line of $13,200.00. Repairable, with $3,945.00 to spare. Verify the Valuation Report: a low ACV moves the line.
6. Loss of Use is claimable for each day without the car or a paid substitute, measured by a comparable rental; Keystone's own $42.00/day rate is the strongest measure. Authority is state law (for example, Restatement (Second) of Torts § 928(b)); confirm the governing rule.
7. Allocate each of the 78 days from 02/06 to 04/24/2026 once; claim only the 48 unpaid: 48 × $42.00 = $2,016.00. A paid rental day claimed twice is a windfall.
8. Keystone's 30-day cap is claims practice; the legal measure generally runs for the reasonable repair time. Keystone blames “shop delay”; the record shows a parts backorder. Arguing the 36 days is for Atty. Thorne.
9. The loss-of-use dates are gap evidence too: no car until the 02/18/2026 rental is the first reason for the 14-Day Gap, and the 03/19 cut-off fed the disrupted care. Keep both records identical, date for date.
10. Keystone's PD coverage ($25,000) pays this claim, outside the $40,000 stack. Bluewater's collision went unused: Keystone accepted PD liability on 02/17/2026.

> ⚠️ **UPL boundary.** You may compute, allocate, document and report PD figures to Atty. Thorne. You may not tell John Doe what his car or its use is worth, whether to accept a Keystone figure, or how a court will measure his damages.

> ⚠️ **UPL boundary.** You may put the PD and rental record in Atty. Thorne's deposition set; you may not rehearse John Doe's answers about the gap or the car.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Show Them

*Every day from 02/06 to 04/24/2026, allocated once*

| Period | Days | What happened | Loss of Use |
|---|---|---|---|
| 02/06–02/17/2026 | 12 | Towed and stored; no car; liability accepted 02/17 | $504.00 claimed |
| 02/18–03/19/2026 | 30 | Rental, $42.00/day: $1,260.00 paid by Keystone | None: already paid |
| 03/20–04/24/2026 | 36 | Rental capped; parts backorder; extension refused 04/10 | $1,512.00 claimed |
| Total | 78 | 48 unpaid days × $42.00; Keystone: $0 | $2,016.00 claimed |

**Operational mapping**

| Trigger Event | ➔ Case Manager Task | ➔ Tool/Interface | ➔ Verification Checkpoint |
|---|---|---|---|
| Final repair bill posts with the supplement | Re-run the total-loss check on $9,255.00 and record the basis | CMS → Property Damage | 52.6% of $17,600.00 recorded; Keystone's 75% basis on file in writing |
| Rental ends with the Civic still in the shop | Log each day without a vehicle, period by period | CMS → Property Damage → Loss of Use | 12 + 36 = 48 days; no paid rental day counted |
| Loss of Use and Diminished Value pleaded in the 09/24/2026 complaint | Reconcile the PD figures across the PD screen, the damages summary and discovery | CMS → Litigation → Pleadings Log | $2,016.00 and $3,600.00 match everywhere; any mismatch escalated to Atty. Thorne |
| John Doe's deposition noticed 01/20/2027 | Add the PD severity set (photos, estimate, 03/02/2026 supplement, rental dates) to Atty. Thorne's deposition documents | CMS → Documents → Deposition set | Indexed before the 02/04/2027 prep session |

**Best practices & pitfalls**

- Run the threshold on the final bill, never the first estimate.
- Use Keystone's own $42.00 rental rate as the value of a day's use; a carrier struggles to dispute its own number.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: claiming the 30 paid rental days again as Loss of Use.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: treating Property Damage as “just the car”; the supplement is impact evidence for the injury claim.

#### 3 · Knowledge Check & CM Summary

Verified in PD Dispute Evaluation (taught on Case Flow Day 5) and that day's auto-graded questions.

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Threshold arithmetic | $6,940.00 + $2,315.00 = $9,255.00; 52.6% of $17,600.00; the 75% line at $13,200.00 |
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Day allocation | 78 days split 12 / 30 / 36, each counted once; 48 × $42.00 = $2,016.00 |
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Doctrine | Make-whole aim, the three damage types and the lesser-of rule stated correctly |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **25%** | Evidentiary reasoning | Connects the supplement and the loss-of-use dates to the injury claim and the gap |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **15%** | UPL boundary | No valuation or acceptance advice to John Doe; the 36-day argument left to Atty. Thorne |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Open with the gauge. Ask which number decides whether the Civic is totaled; most will say the estimate. It is the final bill, $9,255.00, after the supplement.
- Put it to the room: “Keystone paid for 30 days of rental. Why is John Doe still owed anything for the use of his car?” Make them find the two red segments on either side.
- Tie it to Day 5's gap work: the 12 days without a car are the first of the four reasons for the 14-Day Gap, and the 02/18/2026 rental date is in the PD record, not just John Doe's memory. Expect defense counsel to build a causation trap on that fortnight at deposition; the record answers it, and Atty. Thorne, not the CM, prepares John Doe.
- Legal nuance: whether parts-backorder days are recoverable, and how far a carrier's cap binds, vary by jurisdiction. Watch for the 78-day double count; the make-whole principle answers it, and it is not a technicality.
