### 5.1.45 Building the Comparative Liability Counter-Package

> **Skill Activity** · Day 5 — Settlement, closing & the litigation fork · Module 5: Closing & Settlement · scored with Final Comprehensive Knowledge Check (`d5m4`)  
> **Topic:** Skill Activity 1 — answer each allegation from the file  
> **Continuity:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · Atty. Elijah Thorne · $10,000 BI / $20,000 HOST UM / $10,000 REL UM · 14-Day Gap  
> **Theory extracted from:** `D5#23` Critical Litigation Process 3: Third-Party Evidence Subpoenas · `D5#30` Subpoena Duces Tecum: Execution Strategy · LSH Claims Specialist: handling liability disputes — written basis, stronger evidence, witness statements, escalation to the legal team · Newly authored: authentication and chain of custody in the counter-package

#### 1 · Visual Layout Description

A three-row counter-package matrix across the full slide width on a white card with a navy header bar. Columns: Allegation · Answering evidence · Source · Authenticating witness · Chain of custody. Each row opens with an orange numbered badge and a red allegation chip; evidence cells hold mono pills (EDR, EMS, RPD-26-018842, VIDEO). Cells with an open foundation gap are outlined in orange dashes until filled. Beneath, a slim custody timeline for the doorbell video: 02/06/2026 recorded → 02/17/2026 preservation request → 02/19/2026 copy secured → counter-package. Bottom-right, a navy card headed FOR ATTY. THORNE lists the open legal questions. The argument: a row is finished only when its evidence can come in.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Let Them

**Scenario.** File date 11/12/2026, Track B. Atty. Grant Mercer of Mercer & Hale LLP has filed Robert Smith's Answer in Civil Action No. 26-CV-04417. It denies liability and pleads comparative negligence (the Escape entered the all-way stop out of turn; John was unbelted), causation (the gap; a “degenerative” MRI) and failure to mitigate. In the file: report RPD-26-018842, Ruth Calloway's details, the doorbell video, the 10/2026 EDR download, the EMS Run Report and the A-C-T record.

**Execution prompt.** Build the liability counter-package for Atty. Thorne. For each of the three allegations, list the file evidence that answers it, its source document, the witness who can authenticate it, its chain of custody, and any gap in its foundation. Write out the doorbell video's chain from the 02/17/2026 preservation request to the 02/19/2026 copy. For any record that will need a custodian, draft a narrow Exhibit A for a Subpoena Duces Tecum, for his review. Close with the questions only he can answer: the governing fault rule, seatbelt and citation admissibility, and whether fault on Kevin Alvarez touches coverage.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Tool / interface** | CMS → Litigation → Liability counter-package · Action Plan Editor → Subpoena template |
| **Expected output** | An allegation-by-allegation counter-package with foundation for every item, a draft Exhibit A, and the open legal questions routed to Atty. Thorne. |

**Verification checkpoints**

- [ ] Two comparative-fault allegations and the failure-to-mitigate defense listed as separate rows: out of turn, unbelted, failure to mitigate
- [ ] Out of turn answered by report RPD-26-018842 (Smith cited for failure to stop), Ruth Calloway of 2209 Maple Hollow Rd, the doorbell video and the point of impact, with the EDR's 11 mph as corroboration only
- [ ] Unbelted answered by the EDR (front-passenger belt buckled) and the EMS Run Report (“restrained front-seat passenger”)
- [ ] Failure to mitigate answered by the A-C-T record: alert and call 04/08/2026, extension refused 04/10, evening slots from 04/21, attendance confirmed 04/21 and 04/23
- [ ] Disruption stated exactly: 19 days without visits, 04/02 → 04/21/2026, four missed visits, caused by the rental ending and the carpool ending
- [ ] Video chain of custody: Margaret Doe's camera, preservation 02/17/2026, copy secured 02/19/2026, native file unaltered, each transfer logged
- [ ] An authenticating witness named for every item (for example Margaret Doe or Ruth Calloway for the video, a records custodian for the EMS report); whether a signed statement from Ruth Calloway exists is recorded, and taking one is routed to Atty. Thorne
- [ ] Draft Exhibit A limited to John Doe's 02/06/2026 Riverton Fire EMS run and named record types, never “all records”; routing note: notice to Atty. Grant Mercer before the custodian is served
- [ ] Open questions routed to Atty. Thorne: fault regime, seatbelt and citation admissibility, Kevin Alvarez and coverage
- [ ] Stack stated as $40,000 (BI → Host UM → Relative UM); no layer added

**Operational mapping**

| Trigger Event | ➔ Case Manager Task | ➔ Tool/Interface | ➔ Verification Checkpoint |
|---|---|---|---|
| Answer received 11/12/2026 | Open the counter-package, one row per allegation | CMS → Litigation → Liability counter-package | Three rows, each with evidence, source, witness and custody |
| A record will need a custodian | Draft the Subpoena Duces Tecum with a narrow Exhibit A | Action Plan Editor → Subpoena template | Exhibit A names John Doe, 02/06/2026 and specific record types |
| Package complete | Route to Atty. Thorne with the open legal questions | CMS → Tasks → Escalate | Every question listed; no conclusion drafted as advice |

**Best practices & pitfalls**

- One allegation, one row: a reviewer should find the answer to each in seconds.
- Record foundation now; a document no one can authenticate may never reach the jury.
- The EMS report does double duty: “restrained” answers the seatbelt, and the neck complaint on 02/06/2026 answers the gap.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: an Exhibit A asking for “all records of any kind”; it invites a Motion to Quash as a fishing expedition.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: calling the EDR's 11 mph proof of the right-of-way; it corroborates the witness and the video, it doesn't replace them.

#### 3 · Knowledge Check & CM Summary

Verified in Final Comprehensive Knowledge Check (taught on Case Flow Day 5) and that day's auto-graded questions.

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Allegation coverage | All three allegations answered with the right file evidence |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Fact accuracy | RPD-26-018842, 2209 Maple Hollow Rd, buckled and 11 mph, “restrained”, 04/02 → 04/21/2026 and the A-C-T dates exact |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Foundation and custody | Source, witness and chain for every item; video chain 02/17 → 02/19/2026 |
| 🤖 AI | **10%** | Exhibit A scope | Narrow: John Doe, the 02/06/2026 run, named record types |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **20%** | Strategic judgment | Weights the evidence sensibly; doesn't rest liability on the citation or the EDR's speed |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **20%** | UPL boundary | Fault rule, admissibility and coverage all routed to Atty. Thorne; nothing framed as legal advice |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Hand out the Answer's three allegations as three blank rows. Tell the room the package is graded row by row, and a row without an authenticating witness is not finished.
- Ask: “Which single document answers two defenses?” The EMS Run Report: “restrained” answers the seatbelt, and the neck complaint on the day of the crash answers the causation defense on the gap.
- Read the Exhibit A drafts. Anything that says “all records” goes back: one patient, one date, named record types.
- Legal nuance: the EDR's 11 mph is consistent with a car pulling away from a stop, but speed alone does not prove who had the right-of-way. Let Ruth Calloway and the video carry that.

### 5.1.46 Skip-Tracing Execution & Certified Notice Dispatch

> **Skill Activity** · Day 5 — Settlement, closing & the litigation fork · Module 5: Closing & Settlement · scored with Final Comprehensive Knowledge Check (`d5m4`)  
> **Topic:** Skill Activity 2 — find the defendant, reach the client, prove both  
> **Continuity:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · Atty. Elijah Thorne · $10,000 BI / $20,000 HOST UM / $10,000 REL UM · 14-Day Gap  
> **Theory extracted from:** `D5#22` Critical Litigation Processes: Service of Process & E-Filing/Docketing · `D5#29` The Process Server & Evading Defendant Protocol · LSH Claims Specialist: No Contact Protocol; Final Warning Letter; sending Withdrawal Letters (current address, certified mail, dispatch recorded)

#### 1 · Visual Layout Description

Split slide. Left half, PART 1 — SERVICE: a vertical log card (date · time · result) with three failed attempts in red, the Skip-Trace request as an orange escalation chip, the service and affidavit rows in green, ending in a Docket System pill ANSWER DUE 11/13/2026. Right half, PART 2 — CERTIFIED NOTICE: a calendar strip counting backward from 02/18/2027 to 01/22/2027, with 02/04 and 02/16 marked orange as prep sessions and a response-date marker that turns red if it lands after 02/04. Beneath it, the notice draft with a CERTIFIED MAIL + EMAIL chip pair that must both tick, and a navy SKIP-TRACE GATE card with three criteria. The argument: work backward from the date that cannot move.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Let Them

**Scenario.** Two episodes, one file. Part 1, file date 10/08/2026: Allied Process Services reports a third failed attempt at 118 Larkspur Ln (after 10/01 and 10/05), and a neighbor says Robert Smith moved in August. The 10/09 Skip-Trace return (module-local) lists 118 Larkspur Ln (utilities closed 08/2026), 4410 Quarry Ridge Ct, Millbrook (utility opened 08/2026; matches his vehicle registration) and a 2019 address. Part 2, file date 01/22/2027: calls to John Doe go unanswered two days after his deposition was noticed for 02/18/2027, 10:00 a.m., by videoconference. Atty. Thorne has set prep sessions for 02/04 and 02/16/2027.

**Execution prompt.** Part 1: escalate for Skip-Trace authority; evaluate the 10/09 return, choosing the address on recency and two corroborating sources, and record why; re-instruct Allied Process Services; log service 10/14 and the affidavit 10/16, and docket the answer date. Part 2: run Steps 1–3 of the No Contact Protocol with the litigation impact, then draft the Step 4 certified notice for Atty. Thorne's signature: attempts itemized, a 7–14 day response date before 02/04/2027, how to respond. Send it by certified mail AND email to a confirmed address. Graded call: assess John's Skip-Trace gate on Day 3's three criteria, and say why his case is unlike Smith's.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Tool / interface** | CMS → Tasks → Escalate · Docket System · CMS → Communications Log · Action Plan Editor → Warning letter template · CMS → Documents → Outgoing |
| **Expected output** | A service record from Skip-Trace to answer date, and a dispatched certified notice with both deliveries logged and John's Skip-Trace gate assessed. |

**Verification checkpoints**

- [ ] Skip-Trace escalated for authority after the 10/08/2026 attempt, not a fourth knock at 118 Larkspur Ln
- [ ] 4410 Quarry Ridge Ct, Millbrook chosen on two corroborating current sources, 118 Larkspur Ln rejected as stale (utilities closed 08/2026), logged 10/09/2026; server told to vary windows (early morning, evening, weekend where the rules allow; some states bar Sunday service) if the first attempt fails
- [ ] Service 10/14/2026 by Allied Process Services and affidavit filed 10/16/2026 both logged; answer due 11/13/2026 (10/14 + 30 days) docketed
- [ ] Part 2, Step 1 names the live dates: deposition 02/18/2027 (27 days from 01/22), prep 02/04 and 02/16/2027, plaintiff's expert disclosure 03/15/2027
- [ ] Channel ladder logged with date, time, channel and outcome: 2+ calls, email to john.doe@example.com and text, then Margaret Doe as emergency contact (her number tried in Step 2, voicemail left, no return call until 02/03/2027)
- [ ] Step 3 escalation to Atty. Thorne records the deposition impact
- [ ] Final Warning Letter: attempts itemized, a 7–14 day response date before 02/04/2027 (sent by 01/27/2027 for 02/03/2027), how to respond, and the withdrawal line left to Atty. Thorne (possible, never decided)
- [ ] Certified mail with return receipt to 2214 Maple Hollow Rd AND email, both logged; signed by Atty. Thorne, not the CM
- [ ] Withdrawal language present only as Atty. Thorne directed; none from the CM in any other channel, and nothing on what missing the deposition would do to the case
- [ ] Gate assessed on Day 3's three criteria: ladder exhausted (yes, logged); letter sent by certified mail AND email (by 01/27/2027); deadline expired (no: Margaret reached John on 02/03/2027, the response date). Gate not met; no Skip-Trace. Unlike Smith, John's address is known; only his phone changed
- [ ] Margaret Doe told only that the firm needs John to call, and how

**Operational mapping**

| Trigger Event | ➔ Case Manager Task | ➔ Tool/Interface | ➔ Verification Checkpoint |
|---|---|---|---|
| Third failed attempt, 10/08/2026 | Escalate for Skip-Trace authority; re-assign the server to the new address | CMS → Tasks → Escalate · Email Workspace | Skip-Trace result 10/09/2026 logged; varied windows instructed |
| Affidavit of service filed 10/16/2026 | Docket the answer date | Docket System | 11/13/2026 on the calendar with a reminder |
| John unreachable from 01/22/2027 | Run Steps 1–3, then dispatch the Step 4 notice | CMS → Communications Log · CMS → Documents → Outgoing | Both deliveries logged; response date before 02/04/2027 |

**Best practices & pitfalls**

- Log the process server's attempts with date, time and result, exactly as you log your own client attempts.
- Calendar the notice's response date beside the deposition, so one cannot slip past the other.
- A return receipt signed at 2214 Maple Hollow Rd proves delivery to the house, not that John read it: that is why the email goes too.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: a response date that lands after the first prep session.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: “If you don't call us, the firm will withdraw” in a text to John.

#### 3 · Knowledge Check & CM Summary

Verified in Final Comprehensive Knowledge Check (taught on Case Flow Day 5) and that day's auto-graded questions.

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Service record | 10/08 → Skip-Trace 10/09 → served 10/14 → affidavit 10/16 → answer due 11/13/2026, each logged |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Protocol record | Steps 1–3 logged with date, time, channel and outcome; deposition impact recorded |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Notice elements and dates | Attempts itemized, a 7–14 day response date before 02/04/2027, how to respond, and the withdrawal line left to Atty. Thorne (possible, never decided) |
| 🤖 AI | **10%** | Dual delivery | Certified mail and email both logged |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **15%** | Gate judgment | Assesses John's Skip-Trace gate correctly and explains why his case differs from Smith's |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **10%** | Tone | Urgent without threatening; a worried client would call back |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **15%** | UPL and authority boundary | Skip-Trace authority, the signature and any withdrawal decision left with Atty. Thorne; Margaret told nothing of the case |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Run Part 1 as a race against a stale address. The graded move after 10/08 is the Skip-Trace escalation, not a fourth knock.
- Ask the room: “John and Smith both have no working contact point. Why does Smith get a Skip-Trace and John does not?” Day 3's gate: ladder exhausted, letter sent, deadline passed. John was reached on 02/03/2027, the response date, so the deadline never expired; and his address is known, only his phone changed.
- Check every response date against 02/04/2027. A 14-day window from 01/22 lands on 02/05, after the first prep session: the deposition forces the short end of the window.
- UPL gate: no withdrawal language from the Case Manager, in the notice or anywhere else, and Margaret hears only “please have John call us.”

### 5.1.47 Closing a Dropped Case: Withdrawal Letters & the Close-Out Log

> **Skill Activity** · Day 5 — Settlement, closing & the litigation fork · Module 5: Closing & Settlement · scored with Dropped Case Close-Out Check (`d5m6`)  
> **Topic:** Skill Activity — a practice matter: three Withdrawal Letters, their dispatch and the Dropped status  
> **Continuity:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · Atty. Elijah Thorne · $10,000 BI / $20,000 HOST UM / $10,000 REL UM · 14-Day Gap  
> **Theory extracted from:** LSH Claims Specialist: Dropped Cases — sending Withdrawal Letters to clients, carriers and medical providers; the sample letters by email, by text, to a carrier and to a provider · Newly authored: the Denise Harlow practice matter (no health insurance, treatment ended, Pending Drop)

#### 1 · Visual Layout Description

A PRACTICE MATTER banner in orange across the top: DENISE CARVER · LSH-2026-MVA-781205 · NOT JOHN DOE'S FILE. Left half, three stacked letter cards, each with a navy header (CLIENT · GRANITE SHIELD · BROOKSIDE PT) and a method chip (TEXT + EMAIL + CERTIFIED MAIL · EMAIL · FAX); only the client card carries an orange REASON line. Right half, the close-out log as a checklist: gate ✓ 12/08/2026, letters saved WORD + PDF, dispatch 12/09/2026, follow-up 12/16/2026, and a status pill turning from PENDING DROP to DROPPED. Bottom-right, a red chip: “Worthless? Not yours to say.”

#### 2 · Slide Body — Let Them

**Scenario.** A practice matter, not John Doe's file: do not open or create it in the CMS. File date 12/09/2026. Denise Harlow (LSH-2026-MVA-781205) was rear-ended at a red light on 09/19/2026. Granite Shield Insurance holds the BI claim, GS-26-48213 (adjuster Paul Ostrander; CRM note: email only). She treated at Brookside Physical Therapy (CRM note: fax only) from 09/24 to 10/29/2026, then stopped: she has no Health Plan to pay for care, and Brookside declined a Letter of Protection. Atty. Thorne has decided to drop the case. Status: Pending Drop. His note, 12/08/2026, 2:10 p.m.: called Denise, told her the firm will close its representation; she understood; address 87 Alder Creek Dr, Riverton confirmed; she prefers text and email (denise.harlow@mail.example). RS05 shows the firm's standard retainer, with the withdrawal terms every client signs.

**Execution prompt.** Close out the drop in the Action Plan Editor. Record the gate you checked. Draft three Withdrawal Letters for Atty. Thorne's signature: to Denise (date, name, date of loss, the reason in his note's words, and the next step he approves: speak to another attorney promptly, because time limits apply), and one each to Granite Shield and to Brookside (date, client, date of loss, claim number, that the firm no longer represents her; nothing about why). Name each send method and why. Then write the close-out log: Word and PDF saved, each dispatch annotated, the follow-up, the switch to Dropped and the proof retained. Graded judgment: what you say if Denise calls to ask whether her case was worthless.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Tool / interface** | Action Plan Editor → Withdrawal Letter template |
| **Expected output** | Three Withdrawal Letters ready for Atty. Thorne's signature, a send method for each, and a close-out log from Pending Drop to Dropped. |

**Verification checkpoints**

- [ ] Gate recorded: status “Pending Drop” and Atty. Thorne's 12/08/2026 note that Denise Harlow was told; nothing prepared to send before both were confirmed
- [ ] Client letter: 12/09/2026, Denise Harlow, date of loss 09/19/2026, the reason in Atty. Thorne's words (treatment ended 10/29/2026, so the firm cannot continue the claim), the approved next step (speak to another attorney promptly; time limits apply), signed by Atty. Thorne
- [ ] Granite Shield letter: client, claim GS-26-48213, date of loss 09/19/2026, the firm no longer represents her; Brookside letter: patient, date of loss, the firm no longer represents her, please update your records; neither gives the reason
- [ ] Every letter saved in Word and PDF
- [ ] Methods: Denise by text and email (subject “Notice of Withdrawal of Representation”, PDF attached, a brief description) plus Certified Mail with return receipt to 87 Alder Creek Dr; Granite Shield by email and Brookside by fax, per the CRM notes
- [ ] Each dispatch annotated with party, method and date (12/09/2026); each sent letter saved to the file
- [ ] Follow-up on 12/16/2026: the return receipt, Granite Shield's acknowledgement and Brookside's fax confirmation
- [ ] Status switched to “Dropped” only after all three letters are sent, logged and followed up; proof retained: letters, receipts, logs and Atty. Thorne's note
- [ ] If Denise asks whether her case was worthless: no view on its value or on restarting treatment; Atty. Thorne made the decision and will call her; the call logged and routed

**Operational mapping**

| Trigger Event | ➔ Case Manager Task | ➔ Tool/Interface | ➔ Verification Checkpoint |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pending Drop with Atty. Thorne's 12/08/2026 note | Confirm the gate; draft the three letters | Action Plan Editor → Withdrawal Letter template | Client, Granite Shield and Brookside letters, Word and PDF |
| Letters signed, 12/09/2026 | Send each by its method and annotate it | Action Plan Editor → close-out log | Text, email and Certified Mail; email; fax |
| Follow-up, 12/16/2026 | Confirm receipt, then switch the status | Action Plan Editor → close-out log | Dropped; proof retained |

**Best practices & pitfalls**

- Quote Atty. Thorne's reason; never write your own.
- Confirm the mailing address before Certified Mail goes out.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: a carrier letter that says she stopped treating.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: offering Denise a way to “save” the case.

#### 3 · Knowledge Check & CM Summary

Verified in Dropped Case Close-Out Check (taught on Case Flow Day 5) and that day's auto-graded questions.

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **10%** | Gate | Pending Drop and the 12/08/2026 note both confirmed before anything is prepared to send |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Client letter | 12/09/2026, Denise Harlow, 09/19/2026, the reason in Atty. Thorne's words, the approved next step, his signature |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Carrier and provider letters | GS-26-48213 and the date of loss on each; no reason given; “please update your records” to Brookside |
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Methods and close-out log | Text, email and Certified Mail; email; fax; Word and PDF; dispatch logged; follow-up 12/16/2026; Dropped with proof |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **20%** | Judgment | Keeps the reason to the client's letter and the status change behind the follow-up |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **20%** | UPL boundary | No view on the claim's worth or on restarting treatment; Denise routed to Atty. Thorne |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Say it before anyone starts: this is a practice matter. John Doe's case is never dropped, and nothing here goes into his file or the CMS.
- Read three carrier letters aloud. Any that mentions treatment, insurance or the reason goes back: Granite Shield learns only that the firm no longer represents Denise.
- Ask: “Denise calls and asks if her case was worthless. What do you say?” Only Atty. Thorne's decision, his call back and the letter's direction to seek other counsel.

### 5.1.48 The Litigation Net Sheet: Disbursing the Award

> **Skill Activity** · Day 5 — Settlement, closing & the litigation fork · Module 5: Closing & Settlement · scored with Track B Close-Out Check (`d5m5`)  
> **Topic:** Skill Activity — take the award to $14,713.75 on the LSH Net Sheet  
> **Continuity:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · Atty. Elijah Thorne · $10,000 BI / $20,000 HOST UM / $10,000 REL UM · 14-Day Gap  
> **Theory extracted from:** `D3#21` Increasing the Client's Net · `D3#25` The Net Sheet: The LSH Corporate Net Sheet Ledger · `D3#27` Key Takeaway — The Value of a Case Manager · `D3#28` Skill Building: The “Doe v. Apex” Final Net Challenge · Newly authored: the Track B close-out

#### 1 · Visual Layout Description

The LSH Net Sheet fills the slide in its four navy-headed sections. Section 1 reads Gross $40,000.00 with three carrier chips (Keystone, Harbor Point, Cardinal State); Section 2 shows Attorney Fees at 40% = $16,000.00 with an orange SUIT FILED tag and seven receipt rows totalling $3,895.25; Section 3 lists the providers with Peach State reduced to $1,116.00 and Riverton Spine & Rehab to $4,275.00, the zero-balance rows greyed with NOT PAYABLE chips; Section 4 ends on a large orange NET CLIENT DISBURSEMENT $14,713.75.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Let Them

**Scenario.** File date 07/22/2027. Laura Whitcomb's 07/06/2027 award gave Harbor Point's $20,000.00 and Cardinal State's $10,000.00; their checks arrived 07/20 and today, joining Keystone's $10,000.00 held in trust since 03/29/2027: $40,000.00. Peach State accepted $1,116.00 (07/14) and Riverton Spine & Rehab $4,275.00 (07/16), both in writing. Every litigation cost is receipted.

**Execution prompt.** Complete John Doe's LSH Net Sheet for the litigated recovery. Section 1: the gross by carrier. Section 2: the 40% fee the retainer sets once suit is filed, and every receipted cost, pre-suit and litigation, on its row. Section 3: every provider and payer with charges, payments, adjustments and balance owed, and each lien's reduced figure with its Payoff Letter. Section 4: the net to John Doe. Tie it to the Trust Ledger and route the Settlement Statement to Atty. Thorne. Graded judgment: what you explain to John about the difference from a pre-suit settlement, and what you leave to Atty. Thorne.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Tool / interface** | LSH Net Sheet · CMS → Financial Ledger → Trust · CMS → Tasks → Escalate |
| **Expected output** | A Net Sheet reaching $14,713.75, every cost and lien proved, routed to Atty. Thorne before John Doe signs. |

**Verification checkpoints**

- [ ] Gross: Keystone $10,000.00 (mediation, 03/18/2027) + Harbor Point $20,000.00 + Cardinal State $10,000.00 (award, 07/06/2027) = $40,000.00
- [ ] Fee at the 40% tier (suit filed 09/24/2026): $16,000.00, confirmed by Atty. Thorne from the retainer
- [ ] Costs receipted: $486.75 pre-suit + $218.00 filing + $285.00 service + $1,200.00 mediation + $900.00 arbitration + $645.50 court reporter + $160.00 certified records = $3,895.25
- [ ] Liens: Peach State $1,860.00 → $1,116.00 (40% procurement-cost reduction, $744.00, 07/14/2027); Riverton Spine & Rehab $6,290.00 → audited $6,105.00 → $4,275.00 (Payoff Letter 07/16/2027); total $5,391.00
- [ ] Zero-balance lines kept off the payables with their source: MedPay $5,000.00, Crescent's $1,540.00 write-off, the MedPay-paid providers
- [ ] Net: $40,000.00 – $16,000.00 – $3,895.25 – $5,391.00 = $14,713.75; the trust deposits of $40,000.00 tie to the gross
- [ ] Nothing disbursed until both UM checks clear and John Doe signs the Settlement Statement (07/29/2027); disbursement 07/30/2027
- [ ] The difference from a pre-suit settlement explained to John Doe as arithmetic; whether suing was worth it routed to Atty. Thorne

**Operational mapping**

| Trigger Event | ➔ Case Manager Task | ➔ Tool/Interface | ➔ Verification Checkpoint |
|---|---|---|---|
| Last UM check received (07/22/2027) | Post it and complete the Net Sheet | LSH Net Sheet | Gross $40,000.00 · net $14,713.75 |
| Liens accepted in writing | Log the reduced figures and Payoff Letters | LSH Net Sheet · CMS → Documents | Peach State $1,116.00 · Riverton Spine & Rehab $4,275.00 |
| Net Sheet ties to trust | Route the Settlement Statement | CMS → Tasks → Escalate | Routed before John Doe signs |

**Best practices & pitfalls**

- Enter every cost from its receipt, on its row.
- Write each reduction's basis on its line.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: carrying Track A's 33⅓% fee into the litigated file.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: paying a lien from a phone call instead of a Payoff Letter.

**Auto-graded share of the day's Knowledge Check** (5 questions):

1. Keystone offers $3,500 on 08/05/2026. On unreduced liens of $7,965.00, what does John Doe net after the one-third fee and $486.75 in costs?  
   $2,333.33 · $3,013.25 · **✓ $0.00: the $1,846.58 left cannot cover the liens** · $1,846.58  
   *$3,500 – $1,166.67 fee – $486.75 costs = $1,846.58, against $7,965.00 of liens. The offer nets John Doe nothing. That is the math check Atty. Thorne needs before he conveys the offer.*
2. Dana Whitfield calls John Doe's MRI degenerative. Which answer comes straight from the file?  
   **✓ Dr. Helen Park's read: “no significant degenerative change at the adjacent levels.”** · “John Doe has never had chiropractic care of any kind.” · “Some degeneration is normal at 34, so it doesn't matter.” · “If you drop that argument, we can come down from limits.”  
   *The radiologist's own sentence answers it. John Doe did have chiropractic care (three 2021 low-back visits, resolved), and only Atty. Thorne can move off the Limits Demand.*
3. Harbor Point offers $8,000 on the Host UM claim, arguing “BI already compensated him.” Which document answers that?  
   Keystone's Declaration Page of 03/20/2026 · Cardinal State's Coverage Letter of 03/09/2026 · Peach State's reimbursement notice · **✓ Harbor Point's own letter of 03/02/2026: its UM is add-on (excess), not reduced by the BI payment**  
   *On a reduction policy Keystone's $10,000 would come off the $20,000 limit. Harbor Point confirmed in writing that its UM is add-on, so the BI payment does not shrink the $20,000 limit; whether damages fill that room is shown with the medical proof.*
4. Cardinal State tenders its $10,000 Relative UM limits on 10/06/2026. Who accepts it?  
   The Case Manager, because it is the full limit · **✓ John Doe, on his informed decision, conveyed through Atty. Thorne** · Atty. Thorne alone, without troubling the client · Harbor Point, as the primary UM carrier  
   *Settlement is the client's decision. Atty. Thorne advises him and conveys it; the Case Manager logs the decision and drafts the acceptance email for Atty. Thorne to approve.*
5. Before the first call with Dana Whitfield, the battle map sets John Doe's net floor. With $7,965.00 in unreduced liens, $486.75 in costs and the one-third fee, what gross does he need just to reach $0.00 net?  
   $8,451.75 · **✓ $12,677.63** · $10,000.00 · $16,401.40  
   *Liens + costs = $7,965.00 + $486.75 = $8,451.75; × 1.5 for the one-third fee = $12,677.63 gross. That is above Keystone's $10,000 limit, which is why every UM layer and lien reduction matters. $8,451.75 forgets the fee; $16,401.40 is the Specials.*

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Math accuracy | $1,166.67 fee, $486.75 costs, $1,846.58 against $7,965.00, net $0.00 |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Gap rebuttal | Four dated documents; Dr. Park's sentence quoted exactly |
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Baseline and offset | MedPay exhausted and non-subrogated; add-on UM per the 03/02/2026 letter; Relative UM above $30,000; what PIP would change |
| 🤖 AI | **10%** | Completeness | Every question answered substantively |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **15%** | Strategic articulation | The stated next action is the right one and the reasoning holds |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **10%** | Self-Summary quality | Honest and specific, not a restatement of the slide |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **10%** | UPL boundary | No verbal acceptance under pressure; every number and decision routed through Atty. Thorne |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **5%** | Self-rating calibration | Self-score is defensible against demonstrated performance |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Question 1 is the keystone. A trainee who can't show that $3,500 nets John Doe $0.00 has been negotiating without a floor; re-teach the battle map before moving on.
- Put question 6 to the room aloud before anyone writes: “The offer dies at 5:00 p.m. and Atty. Thorne is unreachable. What do you say?” The right answer refuses a verbal yes without rejecting the offer, asks for the offer and its deadline in writing, logs the words, and reaches Atty. Thorne. It never accepts or rejects for John Doe.
- On question 3, listen for the difference between “no PIP here” and “no reduction anywhere.” The file is clean because Harbor Point said so in writing on 03/02/2026, not because the doctrine doesn't exist.
- UPL is a gate: a strong score does not survive an ethics breach.

**Auto-graded share of the day's Knowledge Check** (5 questions):

1. Why can Peach State's $1,860.00 claim be argued down further than a self-funded ERISA plan's usually can?  
   Health Plans have no reimbursement right against an injury recovery · It paid at a contract rate, so it has no lien at all · **✓ It is a fully insured plan, so state law, not ERISA preemption, generally governs its reduction** · MedPay paid first, so the plan's claim is waived  
   *A self-funded ERISA plan answers to federal law and its own terms and is very hard to reduce. A fully insured plan is generally subject to state insurance law, where made-whole and common-fund arguments can apply. Which to argue is Atty. Thorne's call.*
2. Riverton Spine & Rehab's LOP ledger shows $6,290.00. Before asking for any reduction, the Case Manager:  
   **✓ Audits the ledger against the visit records and has the 05/04/2026 duplicate corrected to $6,105.00** · Asks for 50% off the $6,290.00 in the interest of global resolution · Tells John Doe the bill is inflated and he need not pay it · Pays the full balance from trust to keep the provider relationship  
   *Corrections first, reductions second. 34 lines × $185.00 = $6,290.00, but the records show 33 visits; the reduction is negotiated from the audited $6,105.00, never from an inflated bill.*
3. Had only the $10,000 BI been recovered, $6,179.92 would be left after fee and costs. Shared pro rata, Riverton Spine & Rehab's $6,105.00 lien would receive:  
   $6,105.00 · $4,580.00 · $1,443.15 · **✓ $4,736.77**  
   *Riverton holds $6,105.00 of the $7,965.00 in audited liens (about 76.65%), so it takes $6,179.92 × $6,105.00 ÷ $7,965.00 = $4,736.77; Peach State takes $6,179.92 × $1,860.00 ÷ $7,965.00 = $1,443.15. John Doe would net nothing, which is why reductions matter.*
4. Riverton Spine & Rehab's billing manager agrees on a call to accept $4,580.00. The reduction is final when:  
   The call ends with a verbal yes · **✓ Atty. Thorne approves it and the provider's signed Payoff Letter accepts $4,580.00 as full and final satisfaction** · The Case Manager logs the call in the Communications Log · John Doe agrees to the figure  
   *A verbal reduction is not a reduction. No Payoff Letter, no payment: the letter must state full and final satisfaction, and the compromise needs Atty. Thorne's approval.*
5. Peach State paid $1,860.00 on Crescent Pain & Spine's $3,400.00 bill for the 05/15/2026 injection. Building the lien inventory, who can pursue John Doe for the other $1,540.00?  
   Crescent Pain & Spine, as an unpaid provider balance · Peach State, as part of its reimbursement claim · Harbor Point, through MedPay · **✓ Nobody: Crescent took Peach State's contract rate and cannot balance-bill, so the $1,540.00 is recorded as not owed**  
   *The lien is what the plan paid, not what was billed: Peach State asserts $1,860.00. Crescent's $1,540.00 write-off ($3,400.00 – $1,860.00) is a contract-rate adjustment, recorded as not owed. Harbor Point's MedPay has no reimbursement right (03/02/2026).*

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Classification and doctrine | Liens classified and ranked; fully insured status tied to state law; doctrines named correctly |
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Lien math | $1,860.00 – $620.00 = $1,240.00; $1,443.15 + $4,736.77 = $6,179.92; $6,105.00 after the audit |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Written-confirmation steps | Atty. Thorne's approval, the signed Payoff Letter and the ledger update all named |
| 🤖 AI | **10%** | Completeness | Every question answered substantively |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **15%** | Strategic articulation | The stated next action is the right one and the reasoning holds |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **10%** | Self-Summary quality | Honest and specific, not a restatement of the slide |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **10%** | UPL boundary | Escalation to Atty. Thorne identified wherever a figure, a doctrine or a validity question arises |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **5%** | Self-rating calibration | Self-score is defensible against demonstrated performance |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Question 2 is the keystone. A trainee who answers “state law applies, so we argue made whole” has made Atty. Thorne's decision for him; re-teach the line before moving on.
- Question 6 is the pressure scenario. The right answer escalates to Atty. Thorne before any check issues, re-checks how “no prior attorney” was verified, on Atty. Thorne's instruction requests the lawyer's engagement terms and time records in writing, and never treats “one-third” as owed: a discharged lawyer is generally owed the reasonable value of his work, if anything. The disputed amount stays in trust until the claim is resolved (confirm the governing trust rule); the undisputed shares go out only on Atty. Thorne's approval.
- Compare each self-rating with the score. A 5 on a block scored at 60 is a coaching conversation, and so is a 2 on a block they aced.
- UPL is a gate: a strong score does not survive an ethics breach.

> **Topic:** Knowledge Check + Case Manager Self-Summary & Skill Scale  
> **Continuity:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · Atty. Elijah Thorne · $10,000 BI / $20,000 HOST UM / $10,000 REL UM · 14-Day Gap  
> **Theory extracted from:** `D2#37` The Execution: The Dismissal (The “Court” Filing) · `D2#41` Skill Building: BI Settlement — Caught in an Operational Pincer Movement · `D3#23` The Net Sheet: Purpose of a Combined BI/UM Net Sheet · `D3#24` The Net Sheet: Stream 1 (BI) and Stream 2 (UM/UIM) · `D3#27` Key Takeaway — The Value of a Case Manager · `D3#33` Final Case Reconciliation Checklist: Net Sheet (The “Money Trail”) · `D3#34` Final Case Reconciliation Checklist: Proof of Delivery/Completion · `D3#35` Final Case Reconciliation Checklist: Compliance & Identity Verification · `D3#36` Final Case Reconciliation Checklist: The Internal “Closing Memo” · `D3#37` Final Case Reconciliation: Retention & Disposition Schedule · `D3#38` Skill Building: Closing a Case — John Doe v. Apex Delivery Final Tasks

#### 1 · Visual Layout Description

Split layout. Upper half: the knowledge-check panel on the warm cream card with the orange ruled border, questions numbered in orange; beside it, four stacked chips under the heading BEFORE ARCHIVE (MONEY TRAIL · PROOF OF DELIVERY · COMPLIANCE & IDENTITY · CLOSING MEMO), each with an empty tick box. Lower half, below a full-width orange hairline: the CM SELF-SUMMARY on a navy card with white type holding three prompts, beside the 1–5 SKILL SCALE as a five-step horizontal ladder with the competency rows beneath. Far right, the 60/40 split as a stacked bar, 60% AI in navy and 40% Trainer in orange, with UPL as a separate red gate chip below and outside the bar.

#### 2 · Slide Body — Knowledge verification

1. Take John Doe's $40,000.00 to his $20,359.92 net, stream by stream. What does each stream carry for fee, costs and liens, and why is the split 25% / 75%?
2. Name the four document sets that must be in the file before it is archived, the item on John Doe's file that satisfies each, and how long the closed file is kept.
3. The Trust Ledger reads $0.00 after the 10/22/2026 disbursement. Which records prove it, and what would a balance left in trust tell you?

**Operational mapping**

| Trigger Event | ➔ Case Manager Task | ➔ Tool/Interface | ➔ Verification Checkpoint |
|---|---|---|---|
| Ledger at $0.00 (10/22/2026) | Assemble the money trail: every check itemized with its transaction ID, and the zero-balance report | CMS → Financial Ledger → Trust | Bank statement, trust journal and client ledger agree at $0.00, only after all five checks clear |
| Satisfactions and receipt returned | File proof of delivery, the payer-disclosure confirmation and a current W-9 | CMS → Documents | All four sets present before archive |
| Disbursement posted and satisfactions in | Draft the closing letter to John Doe for Atty. Thorne's signature: what was paid, to whom, and the satisfactions received | CMS → Documents → Outgoing | Signed by Atty. Thorne and sent before the file is archived |
| Closing memo written (10/30/2026) | Tag every document by type and docket the disposition date | CMS → Case Summary · CMS → Calendar | Disposition review set 7 years from closing |

**Best practices & pitfalls**

- The four sets before archive: the money trail (itemized checks, transaction IDs, zero balance); proof of delivery (John Doe's signed receipt, lien satisfactions); compliance and identity (a current W-9, not the intake copy, for any 1099 reporting; the payer-disclosure confirmation); the closing memo.
- The payer-disclosure confirmation is John Doe's signed statement that he has disclosed every provider, every bill and every payer (MedPay $5,000.00, Peach State $1,860.00, his own $312.40), so every reimbursement claim is known and the releases' indemnity promise can be kept. It does not say nothing else paid: other sources did, and recovering those bills is generally allowed (collateral source; confirm the governing rule).
- Write the closing memo so a stranger understands the file in two minutes: how it started (a passenger, a 14-Day Gap, a $40,000 stack), how it ended, every discrepancy explained, every exception recorded (or “none granted”). Note that the property-damage claim runs separately and is not closed by it.
- A completion certificate belongs only to repair disbursements: the Civic's repair was paid by Keystone PD directly to the shop and never passed through trust.
- Zero balance proved three ways: the bank statement, the trust journal and John Doe's client ledger agree, and only after all five checks have cleared the bank. An uncashed check is followed up and its funds stay in trust until it clears (confirm the governing reconciliation rule).
- Retention: tag each document by type and apply the firm's schedule (on this training file, 7 years from closing); where a stricter rule applies, it controls. Never destroy financial records before the retention date.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: archiving without John Doe's signed acknowledgment of receipt.
- ⚠️ Pitfall: telling John Doe a late bill is “not our problem.”

#### 3 · Knowledge Check & CM Summary

**Case Manager Self-Summary**

- Summarize, in your own words, what you learned closing John Doe's file: the principles behind the Net Sheet, the release audit and the Trust Ledger, not the clicks.
- Articulate your strategy: the ledger is at $0.00 on 10/22/2026. What is your next action before the 10/30/2026 closing memo, and why is it the right next action rather than something else?
- Identify the one thing in this module you are least confident about, and what you would need in order to be confident.

| Competency | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Building a dual-stream Net Sheet that ties to the Trust Ledger | ○ | ○ | ○ | ○ | ○ |
| Proving each lien's asserted, audited and reduced figures with Payoff Letters | ○ | ○ | ○ | ○ | ○ |
| Auditing a release's parties, claims and indemnity before the client signs | ○ | ○ | ○ | ○ | ○ |
| Running a trust disbursement through its conditions precedent to $0.00 | ○ | ○ | ○ | ○ | ○ |
| Completing the four-set reconciliation, the closing memo and the retention schedule | ○ | ○ | ○ | ○ | ○ |
| Holding the UPL boundary on releases, money and the client's questions | ○ | ○ | ○ | ○ | ○ |

**Auto-graded share of the day's Knowledge Check** (5 questions):

1. Keystone's 08/14 draft release covered Robert Smith, Keystone and “all other persons, firms, corporations and insurers.” What was the danger?  
   It left out the non-admission-of-liability clause · **✓ It would also have released Harbor Point and Cardinal State: $30,000 of the stack** · It had no notary block · It released only the bodily-injury claim  
   *Harbor Point and Cardinal State are insurers. The catch-all line would have released both UM claims; flagged to Atty. Thorne, it was revised to a Limited Release of Robert Smith and Keystone, signed 08/28/2026.*
2. Which of these is a payable line on John Doe's Lien Tracker?  
   Harbor Point MedPay, $5,000.00 · Crescent Pain & Spine's written-off balance · **✓ Riverton Spine & Rehab, $4,580.00** · Riverton Spine & Rehab's duplicate 05/04/2026 visit  
   *MedPay has no reimbursement right (Harbor Point, 03/02/2026); Crescent wrote off its balance and cannot balance-bill; the duplicate was audited out. Only the reduced liens are paid: Riverton Spine & Rehab $4,580.00 and Peach State $1,240.00.*
3. When was the first trust check cut on John Doe's settlement, and why not earlier?  
   09/04/2026, when Keystone's check arrived · 10/13/2026, when the last check arrived · 10/30/2026, with the closing memo · **✓ After the last funds cleared (10/20) and John Doe signed the Settlement Statement (10/21): disbursement 10/22/2026**  
   *Nothing is disbursed against uncleared funds or before the client has signed the statement. All three checks had cleared by 10/20 (the last, Cardinal State's, deposited 10/13), John Doe signed 10/21, and Atty. Thorne signed the checks 10/22.*
4. How are the $486.75 in case costs handled on John Doe's Net Sheet?

5. The Net Sheet shows John Doe netting $20,359.92. Lakeside Orthopedics noted possible repeat ESIs ($3,400–$6,800) ahead. Before John Doe signs, the Case Manager:  
   Holds $6,800.00 of his net in trust to cover the injections · Tells John Doe the net is enough to cover future care · **✓ Flags it to Atty. Thorne: the settlement is full and final and no carrier pays more, so he has that conversation with John Doe** · Adds the injections to Peach State's lien so the plan pays them later  
   *The CM builds and explains the arithmetic but never says whether the net is enough. Future medical was narrated, never in the Specials; with a full and final settlement nothing more comes from Keystone, Harbor Point or Cardinal State. The flag goes to Atty. Thorne.*

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

Model answer: verify the bill and its link to the accident against the ledger and the EOB, route it to Atty. Thorne, ask Crescent in writing to correct its account, and tell John Doe today what you are doing and when he will hear back. Whether he owes it is Atty. Thorne's to say.
- Question 7 is the operational pincer. The right answer stops, documents every contact and escalates at once; any request for more time goes to Keystone in writing on Atty. Thorne's instruction. A deadline is never a reason to let John Doe sign a release that reaches $30,000 of UM coverage.

**Auto-graded share of the day's Knowledge Check** (5 questions):

1. On Track B, why can't the file demand the $20,000 Host UM while Keystone stalls?  
   Harbor Point has already denied the claim · **✓ Host UM opens only once BI is exhausted — paid or tendered at limits — so a UM demand now risks the stack (as configured for this training file; confirm the governing rule)** · UM claims can only be made after a lawsuit is filed · The lapsed Time-Limited Demand bars every other claim  
   *BI → Host UM → Relative UM. Until Keystone pays or tenders its $10,000, the $30,000 of UM behind it stays closed: all $40,000 is frozen behind a $10,000 carrier.*
2. Keystone calls its $3,500 offer “final” on 08/12/2026. Under the “Never” rule, the file:  
   Accepts it as final and tells John Doe · Sends a lower number to keep talks alive · Sends Harbor Point a $20,000 UM demand in parallel · **✓ Doesn't accept “that's all I have” as final: it escalates to the supervisor and makes no move without Atty. Thorne's authority**  
   *Never bid against yourself, and never treat the first “final” as final: every adjuster has a supervisor. The 09/02 escalation is that test, and any new number is Atty. Thorne's to set.*
3. Which statement about the two neutrals is correct?  
   **✓ A mediator cannot force a settlement; an arbitrator's binding award is final, with no appeal on the merits** · A mediator can order Keystone to pay limits if its offer is unreasonable · An arbitrator's award can be appealed like a jury verdict if the math is wrong · Both neutrals issue binding decisions; only the formality differs  
   *The mediator's only tool is persuasion; the arbitrator is a private judge. Courts vacate or correct an award only on narrow grounds, so the Arbitration Binder must be right the first time.*
4. The 09/08/2026 impasse memo to Atty. Thorne should end with:  
   A recommendation to file suit and an estimate of Robert Smith's exposure above his limits · A proposed counter-offer to put to Keystone · **✓ The decision needed from him, framed as questions, with the dated record, the frozen stack and the SOL above it** · A note that John Doe has agreed to go to court  
   *The memo documents and asks. Filing, value and any excess exposure are Atty. Thorne's analysis; the Case Manager's job is a record he can decide from.*
5. Atty. Thorne authorized suit on 09/09/2026, the Case Manager changed the status to Litigation on 09/10, and the Complaint was filed 09/24/2026. From which date does the retainer's 40% fee tier apply?  
   09/09/2026, the authorization · 09/10/2026, the status change · **✓ 09/24/2026, the filing date** · 08/21/2026, the date the demand lapsed  
   *Per the retainer, 33⅓% pre-suit and 40% once suit is filed: the tier follows the filing, not the status. Recording 40% from 09/10 changes John Doe's fee two weeks early. Atty. Thorne discussed the tier with John Doe on 09/09; the CM records that, never explains the fee.*

2. The SOL on John Doe's file is 02/06/2028, a Sunday. The firm's internal filing cutoff is:  
   **✓ 11/05/2027 (Fri)** · 11/08/2027 (Mon) · 02/04/2028 (Fri) · 11/06/2027 (Sat)  
   *The internal hard date is 02/04/2028, the Friday before the Sunday. Ninety days before it is 11/06/2027, a Saturday; counting backward moves to the earlier business day, 11/05/2027. 11/08/2027 is what you get by counting from the Sunday or rolling forward.*
3. The arbitration brief for the 06/22/2027 hearing is due 10 days before. Ten days before is Saturday 06/12/2027. You docket:  
   06/14/2027 (Mon) · 06/12/2027 (Sat) · **✓ 06/11/2027 (Fri)** · 06/02/2027 (Wed)  
   *A backward count that lands on a weekend moves to the earlier business day. Rolling it forward to 06/14 files the brief late; 06/02 is the exhibit exchange.*
4. At the 06/22/2027 hearing, Laura Whitcomb, Esq. asks you directly about a Riverton Spine & Rehab bill. You:  
   Answer briefly: you audited that bill and know it best · Stay silent and wait for Atty. Thorne to notice · Offer to email her the audit after the hearing · **✓ Say you are the Case Manager providing administrative assistance, defer to Atty. Thorne on the record, and hand him the document**  
   *Speaking to the merits before the arbitrator is the unauthorized practice of law, even when your answer is right. Defer politely and put the document in counsel's hands.*
5. The Scheduling Order says mediation is to be “completed by 03/31/2027.” The re-keyed calendar shows “Mediation session 03/31/2027.” The correct fix is:  
   Leave it: the order names the date · **✓ Re-enter it as a deadline, “completed by 03/31/2027”, with a task to collect 3–4 windows from Atty. Thorne, John Doe and opposing counsel before the mediator's office is contacted** · Call Hon. Carla Benton's office and book 03/31/2027 · Roll it forward to 04/01/2027 to leave time for the session  
   *Read each verb in the order: “completed by” sets when the session must be over, not when it is held. Windows are gathered from every party before the neutral hears from you; the session was held 03/18/2027, inside the deadline.*

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Deadline arithmetic | 01/04/2027 with the mail days and the Sunday; brief 06/11/2027 counted backward |
| 🤖 AI | **10%** | SOL cutoffs | 02/04/2028 and 11/05/2027, arithmetic shown |
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Forum logistics | Windows, fee schedule, retainer and layout owned before 06/22/2027; bench directives logged as given and calendared the same day |
| 🤖 AI | **10%** | Completeness | Every question answered substantively |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **15%** | Strategic articulation | The stated next action is the right one and the reasoning holds |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **10%** | Self-Summary quality | Honest and specific, not a restatement of the slide |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **10%** | UPL boundary | The hearing answer defers to Atty. Thorne on the record; the mediation answer escalates rather than accepts |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **5%** | Self-rating calibration | Self-score is defensible against demonstrated performance |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Run question 5 live: play Laura Whitcomb, Esq. and put the bill question to a trainee across the table. Grade the first sentence they say.
- Ask the room: “Which single date on this file would you check twice, and why?” Expect the RFAs or the SOL cutoffs; push for the reasoning.
- Question 3 is the direction test. A trainee who lands on 06/14/2027 has memorised the weekend rule without its direction; re-teach before the binders, which count back from the same hearing.
- UPL is a gate, as on every day: a strong score does not survive an answer that speaks for John Doe at the hearing or tells him whether to take a number.

   *Exhibit numbers are never reshuffled once a set is in other hands, and the CMS master index changes first. An exhibit not exchanged on time may be excluded, so offering it late is Atty. Thorne's decision under the forum's rules.*

   *Cross-reference every provider balance against what the plan already paid (its EOB). Peach State's contract payment satisfied the bill and Crescent wrote off the other $1,540.00 (the $3,400.00 bill less the $1,860.00 paid), so Crescent holds no lien; listing both counts one bill twice against John Doe's recovery.*
5. Sorting the unsorted folder for the 06/22/2027 Arbitration Binder, you find the 09/08/2026 impasse memo to Atty. Thorne. Where does it go?  
   **✓ Flagged for Atty. Thorne's privilege and exchange decision, and kept out of the exchange set** · Tab 6, because it explains the negotiation history · Tab 1 with the submissions, since it shows BI was not paid · Tab 2 with the arbitration pleadings  
   *An internal memo in an exchanged set cannot be unsent. The CM flags; Atty. Thorne decides what is withheld as privileged and what is exchanged. The BI exhaustion threshold is proved with executed documents: Keystone's tender, the UM consents, the Limited Release and Robert Smith's dismissal.*

Thorne (true since the Letter of Representation, and absolute once suit is filed). The CM stops the contact, logs it and tells Atty. Thorne the same day; what happens next is his call.*

   **✓ Discovery Countdown: draft responses reach Atty. Thorne at least 7 days before the court deadline** · Keep a close eye on discovery deadlines · Call the client whenever there is news from the court · Review subpoenas when time allows  
   *A KPI has a formula and a target. For the 01/04/2027 deadline the Discovery Countdown target is 12/28/2026 (7 days before 01/04/2027). Litigation clients are called at least every 30 days, news or not, and subpoenas are audited every 10 days.*

**Auto-graded share of the day's Knowledge Check** (4 questions):

1. John Doe's Civic: final repair $9,255.00, ACV $17,600.00, Keystone's total-loss threshold 75%. The Civic is:  
   A Total Loss: the supplement pushed the repair past half the ACV · **✓ Repairable: $9,255.00 is 52.6% of ACV, under the $13,200.00 line** · A Total Loss: structural damage always totals a car · Repairable only on the $6,940.00 estimate, not on the final bill  
   *$9,255.00 is 52.6% of $17,600.00. The line is 75% × $17,600.00 = $13,200.00, so the Civic is repairable with $3,945.00 to spare. Run it on the final bill, never the first estimate.*
2. Keystone paid John Doe's rental for 02/18–03/19/2026. Which days make up the $2,016.00 loss-of-use claim?  
   **✓ 02/06–02/17 and 03/20–04/24/2026: 48 days without a car or a paid rental** · All 78 days from 02/06 to 04/24/2026 · Only the 30 rental days, at a higher daily rate · Only the 36 days of parts delay  
   *12 + 36 = 48 unpaid days × $42.00 = $2,016.00. The 30 paid days are excluded: claiming them again would pay John Doe twice for the same day.*
3. On John Doe's Civic, what holds Keystone's 17c diminished-value formula to $1,760.00 at most, whatever the damage?  
   The mileage multiplier of 0.60 · The $25,000 PD limit · **✓ The 10% base: 10% of the $17,600.00 ACV, set before any multiplier** · The date of the appraisal  
   *17c starts from 10% of value, $1,760.00 here, and the multipliers can only reduce it. The $3,600.00 appraisal is 20.5% of ACV, a figure the formula can never reach.*
4. Leah Moran tells you Keystone's $792.00 is final and asks you to confirm John Doe will take it. You:  
   Accept it, since anything beats $0 · Reject it and counter at $3,600.00 · Tell John Doe it's a fair offer and let him decide · **✓ Log it, tell her it is with Atty. Thorne, and route it to him the same day**  
   *The CM never makes, accepts, rejects or counters a PD figure, and never values an offer for the client. The CM records and routes; Atty. Thorne decides.*

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Threshold and allocation | $9,255.00 = 52.6% of $17,600.00, under $13,200.00; 48 × $42.00 = $2,016.00 with the 30 rental days excluded |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | 17c and critique | $1,760.00 × 0.75 × 0.60 = $792.00; the cap, the tier and the mileage double count |
| 🤖 AI | **15%** | Doctrine | Three damage types, make-whole, the lesser-of rule, the sentimental-value limit |
| 🤖 AI | **10%** | Completeness | Every question answered substantively |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **15%** | Strategic articulation | The stated next action fits a $5,616.00 against $792.00 dispute and the reasoning holds |

| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **5%** | Self-rating calibration | Self-score is defensible against demonstrated performance |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

   *The gap is substance. Atty. Thorne prepares it with John and chooses what John reviews; the Case Manager supplies no wording and no study document on the gap, and routes the question the same day.*
2. At the deposition, defense counsel asks, “So it was just the neck stiffness. Is that all?” This is:  
   **✓ The “Is that all?” trap: a flat “yes” can be used later against the right-hand numbness and arm symptoms in his records** · A routine personal-background question · An improper question John may refuse to answer · The silent trap  
   *It narrows the injury list to what John recalls on the spot. His Medical Records are the complete account, and Atty. Thorne drills how John meets the question.*
3. Mid-answer, Atty. Thorne says “Objection, form.” John should:  
   Finish his sentence quickly, then pause · **✓ Stop, let the objection finish, then answer unless Atty. Thorne instructs him not to** · Ask the court reporter whether he has to answer · Treat it as an instruction not to answer  
   *An objection is a routine flag on the record. He stops, lets the objection finish, then answers; often he hears “You can answer.” Only “I instruct the witness not to answer” means he does not answer.*
4. Which of the four categories of defense questions do clients most often underreport?  
   Personal background, such as work and residences · The mechanics of the crash at Maple Hollow Rd & Birch Ave · The impact on work and daily life · **✓ Medical history, including anything before the accident**  
   *Past medical history: on this file, the three 2021 low-back chiropractic visits. The defense will likely already have them from the records and its database searches, so the question tests honesty (Golden Rule 1).*

| Competency | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Confirming the Pending Drop gate before anything goes out | ○ | ○ | ○ | ○ | ○ |
| Drafting a Withdrawal Letter for each party | ○ | ○ | ○ | ○ | ○ |
| Choosing and logging each send method | ○ | ○ | ○ | ○ | ○ |
| Closing the file with proof, without advice | ○ | ○ | ○ | ○ | ○ |

**1** Not yet — I need this re-taught · **2** I can follow it with step-by-step help · **3** I can do it with a checklist · **4** I can do it unaided · **5** I can do it unaided and explain it to a peer

**Auto-graded share of the day's Knowledge Check** (3 questions):

1. A practice file shows status “Pending Drop”, but no note from Atty. Thorne or the Case Manager says the client has been told. What happens before any Withdrawal Letter goes out?  
   The client's letter goes by Certified Mail, so it serves as the notice · **✓ Every letter is held until Atty. Thorne confirms, in a note on the file, that the client was told** · The Case Manager calls the client to explain why the firm is dropping the case · The carrier's and provider's letters go now; the client's waits  
   *Two gates come first: the status is Pending Drop AND a note shows the client was told, so the letter is never the client's first news. Telling the client is Atty. Thorne's conversation; nothing goes to any party until both gates are met.*
2. Denise Harlow's three Withdrawal Letters are signed. Which dispatch plan follows the firm's close-out?  
   **✓ Denise by text and email, her preferred methods, plus Certified Mail with return receipt; Granite Shield by email and Brookside Physical Therapy by fax, as the CRM notes say; each dispatch logged, every letter saved in Word and PDF** · Everyone by Certified Mail only, and the status switched to “Dropped” the same day · Denise by text only; Granite Shield and Brookside by phone, logged afterwards · Denise by email, with Granite Shield and Brookside copied, so everyone sees the reason  
   *The client gets her preferred method plus Certified Mail for proof of receipt; carriers and providers get email or fax as the CRM notes say. The reason stays in the client's letter. The status moves to Dropped only once every letter is sent, logged and followed up.*
3. Denise Harlow calls after her letter arrives: “So my case was worthless?” Which answer stays inside the Case Manager's role?  
   “Without treatment there was nothing to claim, so yes.” · “If you find a clinic and restart treatment, the firm may take the case back.” · **✓ “I can't speak to the value of your claim. Atty. Thorne made this decision and will call you to talk it through. Your letter also explains that time limits apply and suggests you speak to another attorney promptly.”** · “The firm thinks your case is too weak, but another lawyer might disagree.”  
   *The Case Manager never values the claim, never re-argues or explains the decision and never advises on next steps. She points Denise only to what Atty. Thorne approved: his call back and the letter's direction to seek other counsel promptly, then logs the call and routes it.*

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Close-out sequence | Gate, letters, methods, log, follow-up and status in order |
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Letter contents and methods | The reason only in the client's letter; each party's method correct |
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Completeness | Every question answered substantively |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **20%** | Strategic articulation | The stated next action is the right one and the reasoning holds |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **20%** | UPL boundary | No view on the claim's worth; the decision left with Atty. Thorne |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- Answers come from the practice matter: Denise Harlow, 12/08 and 12/09/2026, Granite Shield by email, Brookside by fax.
- The UPL gate: a trainee who explains the drop to Denise, or tells her what to do next, has crossed the line, whatever the rest of the answer.

### 5.1.61 Track B Close-Out Check

> **Knowledge Check** · Day 5 — Settlement, closing & the litigation fork · Module 5: Closing & Settlement · scored with Track B Close-Out Check (`d5m5`)  
> **Topic:** Knowledge Check + Case Manager Self-Summary & Skill Scale  
> **Continuity:** John Doe v. Robert Smith · Atty. Elijah Thorne · $10,000 BI / $20,000 HOST UM / $10,000 REL UM · 14-Day Gap  
> **Theory extracted from:** `D3#23` The Net Sheet: Purpose of a Combined BI/UM Net Sheet · `D3#24` The Net Sheet: Stream 1 (BI) and Stream 2 (UM/UIM) · `D3#27` Key Takeaway — The Value of a Case Manager · Newly authored: the Track B close-out

#### 1 · Visual Layout Description

**Auto-graded share of the day's Knowledge Check** (2 questions):

1. Track B recovers the same $40,000.00 gross as Track A. Why does John Doe net $14,713.75 instead of $20,359.92?  
   The arbitrator reduced the award for the 14-Day Gap · **✓ Suit was filed: the fee moved to 40% ($16,000.00) and costs rose to $3,895.25** · The UM carriers paid less than their limits · Peach State's lien grew with interest  
   *Same gross, different deductions: 40% once suit was filed ($16,000.00 against $13,333.33), $3,895.25 in receipted costs against $486.75, and liens of $5,391.00 against $5,820.00. $40,000.00 – $16,000.00 – $3,895.25 – $5,391.00 = $14,713.75.*
2. Peach State asserts $1,860.00 against the litigated fund. What does the procurement-cost reduction at this file's fee produce?  
   $1,240.00 · **✓ $1,116.00** · $744.00 · $1,860.00  
   *The reduction follows the fee actually charged: 40% of $1,860.00 is $744.00, so $1,860.00 – $744.00 = $1,116.00, accepted in writing 07/14/2027. Track A's $1,240.00 reflected the 33⅓% pre-suit fee.*

#### 4 · Assessment Criteria (AI + Trainer Rubric)

| Channel | Weight | Dimension | What is graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Net Sheet arithmetic | $40,000.00 → $14,713.75 with every deduction and its source |
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Fee and lien doctrine | The 40% tier from the retainer; reductions at the fee actually charged |
| 🤖 AI | **20%** | Completeness | Every question answered substantively |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **20%** | Strategic articulation | The stated next action is the right one and the reasoning holds |
| 🧑‍🏫 Trainer | **20%** | UPL boundary | No opinion on the worth of the suit, the fee or the award |

**Composite** = AI × 0.60 + Trainer × 0.40. **UPL is a gate, not a weighting:** giving the client legal advice fails the block's UPL criterion whatever the composite.

#### 5 · Speaker Notes

- This is the last check of the Case Flow: the file is closed from intake to award.
- Question 5 is the UPL gate: arithmetic yes, opinion no. A trainee who says the lawsuit was or wasn't worth it has crossed the line.
